\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(g_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\gamma\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(g_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\gamma\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle=T_{\varepsilon}+\mathtt{T}_{D}+\mathtt{T}_{E},

where in the last line we used the definition of
T_{\varepsilon}
and defined
\mathtt{T}_{D}
and
\mathtt{T}_{E}
respectively, which we bound separately below.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{D}
.

From
Lemma
14
recall that
\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-g_{t}(x_{t})\|\leq\Delta_{e}(x_{t})
. Thus, for any
x_{t}
for which
\|g_{t}(x_{t})\|\leq\Delta_{e}(x_{t})
, Lemma
13
implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq 2\gamma\|f_{t}(x_{t})-{f^{\star}}(x_{t})\|+\mathtt{Margin}(g_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\gamma\Delta_{t}(x_{t})+\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t})).

The above implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{D}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(g_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\gamma\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq 4\gamma\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle\leq 0,

where the last line follows from the fact that all the conditions inside the indictor can not hold simultaneously for any
\varepsilon>0
.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{E}
.
We note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(g_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\gamma\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\geq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Delta_{e(t)}(x_{t})\geq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}\right\}.

Using Lemma
16
with
\zeta=\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4\gamma}}
to bound the term on the right hand side above, we get that with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\log(T)\gamma^{2}\cdot\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{\Psi}^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F};T)}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\theta^{\mathrm{val}}\left(\mathcal{F},\frac{\varepsilon}{8\gamma},\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{\Psi}^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F};T)}}{T};f^{\star}\right)+\log(2/\delta)\right).

Gathering the bounds above, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}\leq T_{\varepsilon}+O\left(\log(T)\gamma^{2}\cdot\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{\Psi}^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F};T)}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\theta^{\mathrm{val}}\left(\mathcal{F},\frac{\varepsilon}{8\gamma},\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{\Psi}^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F};T)}}{T};f^{\star}\right)+\log(2/\delta)\right).

Since
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above, the final bound follows by choosing the best parameter
\varepsilon
, and by plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{\Psi}^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F};T)}
.

C.3
Proof of Corollary
1

Note that the Tsybakov noise condition implies that there exists constants
c,\rho\geq 0
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\Pr_{x\sim\mu}\left(\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\right)\leq c\varepsilon^{\rho}.

Thus, using Lemma
4
, we get that

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3T}{2}\Pr_{x\sim\mu}\left(\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x))\leq\varepsilon\right)+4\log(2/\delta)

\displaystyle\leq 2cT\varepsilon^{\rho}+4\log(2/\delta).

Using the above in the bound for Theorem
2
, we get that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim cT\varepsilon^{\rho+1}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\lambda\varepsilon}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}+\log(1/\delta),

Setting
\varepsilon=\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\lambda CT}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}\right)^{\frac{1}{\rho+2}}
in the above implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\lambda}c^{\frac{1}{\rho+1}}\right)^{\frac{\rho+1}{\rho+2}}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}\right)^{\frac{\rho+1}{\rho+2}}\cdot(T)^{\frac{1}{\rho+2}}+\log(1/\delta).

Similarly, we can bound the query complexity bound for any
\varepsilon>0
as:

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\varepsilon^{\rho}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}\cdot\theta^{\mathrm{val}}\left(\mathcal{F},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{8\gamma}},\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}}}{{T}};f^{\star}\right)+\log(1/\delta).

Setting
\varepsilon=\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\lambda T}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}\cdot\theta^{\mathrm{val}}\left(\mathcal{F},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{8\gamma}},\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}}}{{T}};f^{\star}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{\rho+2}}
in the above implies that

\displaystyle N_{T}\leq\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\lambda}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}\cdot\theta^{\mathrm{val}}\left(\mathcal{F},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{8\gamma}},\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F};T)}}}{{T}};f^{\star}\right)\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\rho+2}}\cdot T^{\frac{2}{\rho+2}}.

C.4
Proof of Theorem
4

Before delving into the proof, we recall the relevant notation. In Algorithm
2
,

\bullet

The label
y_{t}\sim{f^{\star}}(x_{t})
as given in
(
2
)
.

8
8

8

Throughout the proof of Theorem
4
, we use the link function
\phi(z)=z
, and observe that
\lambda=\gamma=1
for this choice of
\phi
. This corresponds to
\ell_{\phi}
being the square loss.

\bullet

The function
\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t}(x_{t}))=\argmax_{k}f_{t}(x_{t})[k]
.

\bullet

For any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
, the margin is given by the gap between the value at the largest and the second largest coordinate, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathtt{Margin}(v)=v[k^{\star}]-\max_{k\neq k^{\star}}v[k],

where
k^{\star}\in\argmax_{k\in[K]}v[k]
.

\bullet

We also define
T_{\varepsilon}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}
to denote the number of samples within
T
rounds of interaction for which the margin w.r.t.
{f^{\star}}
is smaller than
\varepsilon
.

\bullet

The Inverse Gap Weighting (IGW) distribution
p_{t}=\mathrm{IGW}_{\varepsilon}(f_{t}(x_{t}))
is given by:

\displaystyle p_{t}[y]=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{K+\varepsilon\left(f_{t}(x_{t})[y_{t}^{\star}]-f_{t}(x_{t})[y]\right)}&\text{if}\penalty\ y\neq y_{t}^{\star}\\
1-\sum_{y\neq y_{t}^{\star}}p_{t}[y]&\text{if}\penalty\ y=y_{t}^{\star}\end{cases},

where
y_{t}^{\star}=\argmax f_{t}(x_{t})[y]
.

\bullet

We define the function
\mathrm{Gap}:\mathbb{R}^{K}\times[K]\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{+}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gap}(u,k)=\max_{k^{\prime}}u[k^{\prime}]-u[k].

(45)

to denote the gap between the largest and the
k
-th coordinate of
v
.

\bullet

For ease of notation, we define the functions
{\pi^{\star}}
such that

\displaystyle{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\argmax_{k}\phi({f^{\star}}(x))[k].

C.4.1
Supporting Technical Results

Lemma 17
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, the function
{f^{\star}}\in\mathcal{F}
satisfies the following for all
t\leq T
:

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-{f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}])^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}=2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}+8\log(T/\delta)
. Thus, for all
t\leq T
,
{f^{\star}}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
.

Proof.

We first note that we do not query oracle when
Z_{s}=0
. Hence, we will ignore the terms for which
Z_{s}=0
. Now note that the regret guarantee for the oracle, given in
(
9
)
ensures that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-\mathbb{Q}_{s})^{2}-\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}({f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-\mathbb{Q}_{s})^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}.

Taking expectation on both sides yields

\displaystyle\hskip-72.26999pt\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-\mathbb{Q}_{s})^{2}-\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}({f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-\mathbb{Q}_{s})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}\Big((f_{s}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}])^{2}-2\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{s}\cdot f_{s}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]+2\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{s}\cdot{f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-({f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}])^{2}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-{f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}])^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}

where in the third line we use the fact that
\En\left[\mathbb{Q}_{s}\right]={f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]
. Applying the concentration result (Lemma
4
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}]-{f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widehat{y}_{s}])^{2}\leq 2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}+8\log(1/\delta).

We complete the proof by taking a union bound for all
t\in[T]
.
∎

For the rest of the proof, we condition on the
1-\delta
probability event that Lemma
17
holds. We first recall the following property of the Inverse Gap Weighting (IGW) sampling distribution.

Lemma 18

(
Foster and Rakhlin 2020
)
.

Let
K\geq 2
,
u,v\in[0,1]^{K}
, and suppose
\widehat{y}\sim\mathrm{IGW}_{\varepsilon}(v)
. Then, we have

\En_{\widehat{y}}\left[{\max_{y}u[y]-u[\widehat{y}]}\right]\leq\frac{K}{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\En_{\widehat{y}}\left[\left(u[\widehat{y}]-v[\widehat{y}]\right)^{2}\right].

Lemma 19
.

Let
{f^{\star}}
satisfy Lemma
17
, and let
w_{t}
be defined in
(
10
)
in Algorithm
2
. Then, for any
\zeta>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\geq\zeta\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\zeta^{2}}+1\right)\check{\mathfrak{E}}(\mathcal{F},\zeta/2;f^{\star}).

where
\check{\mathfrak{E}}
denotes the eluder dimension given in Definition
8
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\hskip-72.26999pt\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\geq\zeta\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\geq\zeta/2\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\zeta^{2}}+1\right)\check{\mathfrak{E}}(\mathcal{F},\zeta/2;f^{\star}).

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
7
.
∎

Lemma 20
.

For any
t\in[T]
, assume
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
and
\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon
. Then we have
w_{t}\geq\varepsilon/2
whenever
|\mathcal{A}_{t}|>1
.

Proof of Lemma
20
.

When
|\mathcal{A}_{t}|>1
, we know there exists a function
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
for which the maximizer of
f^{\prime}(x_{t})
, denoted by
y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
, differs from
\pi^{\star}(x_{t})
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[y^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]+f^{\prime}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[y^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\leq f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]+f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y^{\prime}]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[y^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\leq 2w_{t}

from which we conclude
w_{t}\geq\varepsilon/2
.
∎

Lemma 21
.

Assume
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
for all
t\in[T]
. Suppose there exists some
t^{\prime}\in[T]
such that
\xi_{t}=0
for all
t\leq t^{\prime}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}w_{t}\leq T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon)),

for any fixed
\varepsilon
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

We first note that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}w_{t}=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}w_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\right\}+\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}w_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon\right\}.

The first term is exactly
T_{\varepsilon}
. Now we consider the second term. Since
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
, we always have
\pi^{\star}(x_{t})\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
for all
t\in[T]
. Hence, whenever
Z_{t}
is zero, we have
\mathcal{A}_{t}=\{\pi^{\star}(x_{t})\}
and thus we do not incur any regret. Hence, we know
Z_{t}w_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon\right\}
is either 0 or at least
\varepsilon/2
by Lemma
20
. We denote
\varepsilon^{\prime}\coloneqq\varepsilon/2
for notational simplicity. Let us fix an integer
m>1/\varepsilon^{\prime}
, whose value will be specified later. We divide the interval
[\varepsilon^{\prime},1]
into bins of width
1/m
and conduct a refined study:

\displaystyle\hskip-72.26999pt\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}w_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}Z_{t}w_{t}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left\{Z_{t}w_{t}\in\left[\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m},\,\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{w_{t}\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{y\in\mathcal{A}_{t}}\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y]\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}\sup_{y\in\mathcal{A}_{t}}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y]\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}\sum_{y}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y]\geq\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}\right)K\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim p_{t}}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y]\geq\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right)\right\}}_{(*)}

where in the third inequality we replace the supremum over
a
by the summation, and in the last inequality we further replace it by the expectation. Here recall that
p_{t}(a)
is uniform when
\xi_{t}=0
, leading to the extra
K
factor. To deal with
(*)
, we first apply Lemma
4
to recover the empirical
a_{t}
, and then apply Lemma
19
to get an upper bound via the eluder dimension:

\displaystyle(*)\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\geq\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right)\right\}+8\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right)^{2}}+1\right)\check{\mathfrak{E}}(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star})+8\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{10{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right)^{2}}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)+8\log(\delta^{-1})

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Plugging
(*)
back, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}w_{t}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}\right)\cdot\frac{10K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right)^{2}}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)+8mK\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 10K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j+1}{m}}{\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}\right)^{2}}+8mK\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 10K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}+1/m}{\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}}\right)+8mK\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 10K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{j}{m}}+8mK\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 20K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{\left(1-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)m-1}\int_{j-1}^{j}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{x}{m}}\mathrm{d}x+8mK\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 20K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)\int_{-1}^{(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})m-1}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\prime}+\frac{x}{m}}\mathrm{d}x+8mK\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 20K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)\cdot m\log\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\prime}-m^{-1}}\right)+8mK\log(\delta^{-1})

where for the second inequality, we use the fact that
(j+1)/m\leq 2j/m
for any
j\geq 1
; for the third inequality, we assume
m>1/\varepsilon^{\prime}
. Setting
m=2/\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}w_{t}\leq

\displaystyle 40K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\log(2/\varepsilon^{\prime})+16K\log(\delta^{-1})/\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 56K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon^{\prime}/2;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\log(2/(\delta\varepsilon^{\prime})),

which completes the proof. Recall that we have set
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon/2
.
∎

Lemma 22
.

For any
t\in[T]
and any arm
y\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
, we have
f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[y]\leq 2w_{t}
.

Proof of Lemma
22
.

For any
y\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
, by the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{t}
, there must exists a function
f
for which
y
is the maximizer of
{f}(x_{t})
. Hence,

\displaystyle\hskip-72.26999ptf^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[y]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f(x_{t})[y]+f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[y]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]+f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[y]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2w_{t}.

∎

Lemma 23
.

Whenever

T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon))<\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}},

we have
\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}=\dots=\xi_{T}=0
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof of Lemma
23
.

We prove it via contradiction. Assume the inequality holds but there exists
t^{\prime}
for which
\xi_{t^{\prime}}=1
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\xi_{t}=0
for all
t<t^{\prime}
, namely that
t^{\prime}
is the first time that
\xi_{t^{\prime}}
is 1. Then by definition of
\xi_{t^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}Z_{s}w_{s}\geq\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}.

On the other hand, by Lemma
21
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}Z_{s}w_{s}\leq T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon)).

The combination of the above two inequalities contradicts with the conditions.
∎

C.4.2
Regret Bound

We begin by showing the worst-case regret upper bound.

Worst-Case Regret Upper Bound.

We observe the definition of
\xi_{t}
in Algorithm
2
: the left term
\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s}w_{s}
in the indicator is non-decreasing in
t
while the right term remains constant. This means that there exists a particular time step
t^{\prime}\in[T]
dividing the time horizon into two phases:
\xi_{t}=0
for all
t\leq t^{\prime}
and
\xi_{t}=1
for all
t>t^{\prime}
. Now, we proceed to examine these two phases individually.

For all rounds before
t^{\prime}
, we can compute the expected partial regret as

\displaystyle\hskip-72.26999pt\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}\Pr\left(\widehat{y}_{t}\neq y_{t}\right)-\Pr\left({\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}\big(1-\Pr\left(\widehat{y}_{t}=y_{t}\right)\big)-\big(1-\Pr\left({\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})=y_{t}\right)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}\big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}Z_{t}\Big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}}2Z_{t}w_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{KT{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}

(46)

where the third equality holds since we have
\mathcal{A}_{t}=\{\pi^{\star}(x_{t})\}
whenever
Z_{t}=0
under the condition that
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
. The first inequality is Lemma
22
, and the second inequality holds by the definition of
\xi_{t}
and the condition that
\xi_{t}=0
.

On the other hand, for all rounds after
t^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=t^{\prime}+1}^{T}\Pr\left(\widehat{y}_{t}\neq y_{t}\right)-\Pr\left({\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=t^{\prime}+1}^{T}Z_{t}\Big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=t^{\prime}+1}^{T}Z_{t}\left(\frac{K}{\alpha_{t}}+\alpha_{t}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{a,b\sim p_{t}}\Big[\big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[y]-f_{t}(x_{t})[y]\big)^{2}\Big]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=t^{\prime}+1}^{T}Z_{t}\left(\frac{K}{\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}}+\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{a,b\sim p_{t}}\Big[\big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[y]-f_{t}(x_{t})[y]\big)^{2}\Big]\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{KT{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}+\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}+1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbb{E}_{a,b\sim p_{t}}\Big[\big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[y]-f_{t}(x_{t})[y]\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{KT{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}+\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}+1}^{T}Z_{t}\big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]-f_{t}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\big)^{2}+8\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{KT{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}+\sqrt{KT{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}+8\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1}).

(47)

where the first inequality holds by Lemma
18
, the second equality is by the definition of
\alpha_{t}
, the third inequality is by Lemma
4
, and the last inequality holds by our assumption on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}
.

Combining the two parts, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pr\left(\widehat{y}_{t}\neq y_{t}\right)-\Pr\left({\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)\leq 12\sqrt{KT{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1}).

(48)

Instance-Dependent Regret Upper Bound.

We fix any
\varepsilon>0
and consider two cases. First, when

T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon))<\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}},

(49)

we invoke Lemma
23
and get that
\xi_{t}=0
for all
t\in[T]
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pr\left(\widehat{y}_{t}\neq y_{t}\right)-\Pr\left({\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\Big(f^{\star}(x_{t})[\pi^{\star}(x_{t})]-f^{\star}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}w_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}\cdot 12{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1})+1344K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{2}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log^{2}(4/(\delta\varepsilon))

where the first inequality is by Lemma
22
and the fact that we incur no regret when
Z_{t}=0
since
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
. The second inequality is by Lemma
21
.

On the other hand, when the contrary of (
49
) holds, i.e.,

T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon))\geq\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}},

(50)

applying the worst-case regret upper bound
(
48
)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\leq

\displaystyle 12\sqrt{KT{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 12{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1})\cdot\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}\cdot 12{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1})+12{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1})\cdot 112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}\cdot 12{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\log(4\delta^{-1})+1344K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{2}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log^{2}(4/(\delta\varepsilon))

where we apply the condition (
50
) in the second inequality.

Hereto, we obtain the instance-dependent upper bound for regret in both cases given a fixed
\varepsilon
. Recall that the choice of
\varepsilon
is arbitrary. Hence, we can take the infimum on
\varepsilon
and complete the proof.

C.4.3
Total Number of Queries

The upper bound of
T
is trivial since our algorithm clearly will not make more than
T
queries. Now we prove the instance-dependent upper bound. We first fix an arbitrary
\varepsilon
.

We also consider two cases. First, when

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon))<\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}},

(51)

we can invoke Lemma
23
and get that
\xi_{t}=0
for all
t\in[T]
. Hence,

\displaystyle N_{T}=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}=T_{\varepsilon}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{w_{t}\geq\varepsilon/2\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\sup_{y\in\mathcal{A}_{t}}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y]\geq\varepsilon/2\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{A}_{t}}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y]\geq\varepsilon/2\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+K\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim p_{t}}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[y]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[y]\geq\varepsilon/2\right\}}_{(*)}

where the second equality is by Lemma
20
, the last step holds as
p_{t}(a)
is uniform for any
a
when
\xi_{t}=0
. We apply Lemma
3
and Lemma
19
to
(*)
and obtain

\displaystyle(*)\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}f(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]-f^{\prime}(x_{t})[\widehat{y}_{t}]\geq\varepsilon/2\right\}+8\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{(\varepsilon/2)^{2}}+1\right)\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)+8\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{10{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{(\varepsilon/2)^{2}}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)+8\log(\delta^{-1}).

Plugging this back, we obtain

\displaystyle N_{T}\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{10K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{(\varepsilon/2)^{2}}\cdot\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)+8K\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2T_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}/K+25088K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{3}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log^{2}(4/(\delta\varepsilon)).

On the other hand, when the contrary of (
51
) holds, i.e.,

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}+112K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\cdot\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon}\cdot\log(4/(\delta\varepsilon))\geq\sqrt{KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}.

Squaring both sides and using the fact that
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
for any
a,b
, we obtain

\displaystyle 2T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+25088K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{2}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log^{2}(4/(\delta\varepsilon))\geq KT/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}

which leads to

T\leq 2T_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}/K+25088K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{3}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log^{2}(4/(\delta\varepsilon)).

We note that we always have
N_{T}\leq T
, and thus,

N_{T}\leq T\leq 2T_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}/K+25088K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{3}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}\frac{\check{\mathfrak{E}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon/4;f^{\star}\right)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log^{2}(4/(\delta\varepsilon)).

Hence, we complete the proof for a fix
\varepsilon
. Recall that the choice of
\varepsilon
is arbitrary. Hence, we can take the infimum and thus complete the proof.

C.5
Proof of Theorem
5

In this section, we consider selective sampling with bandit feedback where on every query, the learner chooses two actions
\widehat{y}_{t}
and
\widetilde{y}_{t}
and receives back bandit feedback

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}_{t}=\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}_{t}=y_{t}\},\qquad\text{and,}\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{t}=\mathbf{1}\{\widetilde{y}_{t}=y_{t}\},

where
y_{t}
is the action chosen by the noisy expert. In Theorem
5
, we consider the link function
\phi(z)=z
corresponding to which, we have
\ell_{\phi}(u,v)=(u-v)^{2}/2
denotes the square loss, and
\lambda=\gamma=1
. We present our selective sampling algorithm in Algorithm
5
, and note that it builds on access to a square-loss regression oracle
\mathsf{Oracle}
.

In the following, we recall the other notation used in Algorithm
5
,

\bullet

The label
y_{t}\sim{f^{\star}}(x_{t})
as given in
(
2
)
.

\bullet

The function
\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t}(x_{t}))=\argmax_{k}f_{t}(x_{t})[k]
.

\bullet

For any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
, the margin is given by the gap between the value at the largest and the second largest coordinate, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathtt{Margin}(v)=v[k^{\star}]-\max_{k\neq k^{\star}}v[k],

where
k^{\star}\in\argmax_{k\in[K]}\phi(v)[k]
.

\bullet

For any
\varepsilon>0
, we define
T_{\varepsilon}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}
to denote the number of samples within
T
rounds of interaction for which the margin w.r.t.
{f^{\star}}
is smaller than
\varepsilon
.

\bullet

We define the function
\mathrm{Gap}:\mathbb{R}^{K}\times[K]\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{+}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gap}(u,k)=\max_{k^{\prime}}u[k^{\prime}]-u[k].

(52)

to denote the gap between the largest and the
k
-th coordinate of
v
.

Algorithm 5

Selective Sampling with Multiple Bandit Queries

1:

Parameters
\delta,\gamma,\lambda
,
T
, function class
\mathcal{F}
, and online square loss regression oracle
\mathsf{Oracle}
.

2:

Define
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}=2K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}+8K\log(T/\delta).

3:

Learner computes
f_{1}\leftarrow\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}_{1}(\emptyset)
.

4:

for

t=1
to
T

do

5:

Nature chooses
x_{t}
.

6:

Learner plays the action
\widehat{y}_{t}=\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t}(x_{t}))
.

7:

Learner computes

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}_{t}(x_{t})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon={}\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\penalty\ \left\|f(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\right\|^{2},\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s}\left\|f(x_{s})-f_{s}(x_{s})\right\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}.\qquad

(53)

8:

Learner decides whether to query:
Z_{t}=\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\}
.

9:

if

Z_{t}=1

then

10:

Learner chooses the exploration action
\widetilde{y}_{t}\sim\mathrm{Uniform}([K])
.

11:

Learner queries for the actions
\widehat{y}_{t}
and
\widetilde{y}_{t}
and receives back the bandit feedback

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}_{t}=\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}_{t}=y_{t}\},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{t}=\mathbf{1}\{\widetilde{y}_{t}=y_{t}\}.

12:

f_{t+1}\leftarrow\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}_{t}(\{(x_{t},\widetilde{y}_{t}),\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{t}\})
.

13:

else

14:

Learner plays the action
\widehat{y}_{t}=\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t}(x_{t}))
.

15:

f_{t+1}\leftarrow f_{t}
.

C.5.1
Supporting Technical Results

Lemma 24
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, the function
{f^{\star}}\in\mathcal{F}
satisfies the following for all
t\leq T
:

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}\|f_{s}(x_{s})-{f^{\star}}(x_{s})\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}=2K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}+8K\log(T/\delta)
. Thus, for all
t\leq T
,
{f^{\star}}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
.

Proof.

We first note that we do not query oracle when
Z_{s}=0
. Hence, we will ignore the terms for which
Z_{s}=0
. Now note that the regret guarantee for the oracle, given in
(
9
)
ensures that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]-\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{s})^{2}-\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}({f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]-\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{s})^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}.

Taking expectation on both sides yields

\displaystyle\hskip-72.26999pt\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]-\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{s})^{2}-\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}({f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]-\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{s})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}\Big((f_{s}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}])^{2}-2\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{s}\cdot f_{s}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]+2\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{s}\cdot{f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]-({f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}])^{2}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}(f_{s}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]-{f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}])^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}\|f_{s}(x_{s})-{f^{\star}}(x_{s})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}

where in the third line we use the fact that
\En\left[\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{s}\right]={f^{\star}}(x_{s})[\widetilde{y}_{s}]
, and the fourth line follows from the fact that
\widetilde{y}_{s}\sim\mathrm{Uniform}([K])
. Applying the concentration result (Lemma
4
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}Z_{s}\|f_{s}(x_{s})-{f^{\star}}(x_{s})\|^{2}\leq 2K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{sq}}(\mathcal{F};T)}+8K\log(1/\delta).

We complete the proof by taking a union bound for all
t\in[T]
.
∎

For the rest of the proof, we condition on the
1-\delta
probability event that Lemma
24
holds. Additionally, we will use Lemma
11
and Lemma
12
from the proof of Theorem
1
in Appendix
C.1
, which we will invoke by setting
\gamma=1
for the link function
\phi(z)=z
.

We finally note the following technical lemma which allows us to bound the number of times for which we query for the label and
\Delta_{t}(x_{t})
is larger than a constant in terms of the eluder dimension.

Lemma 25
.

Let
{f^{\star}}
satisfy Lemma
24
, and let
\Delta_{t}(x_{t})
be defined in
(
53
)
in Algorithm
5
. Then, for any
\zeta>0
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\geq\zeta\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\zeta^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F},\nicefrac{{\zeta}}{{2}};{f^{\star}}).

where
\mathfrak{E}
denotes the eluder dimension given in Definition
1
.

The proof of Lemma
25
is identical to the proof of Lemma
11
, so we skip it for conciseness.

C.5.2
Regret Bound

For the ease of notation, through the proof, we define the function
{\pi^{\star}}
such that

\displaystyle{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\argmax_{k}{f^{\star}}(x)[k].

Furthermore, recall that
\widehat{y}_{t}
denotes the action chosen by the learner at round
t
of interaction. The proof follows along the lines of the proof of Theorem
1
in Appendix
C.1
. Let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. Following the identical steps till
(
25
)
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}\cdot\varepsilon

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}_{t}\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}\cdot\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{Z}_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}_{t}\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\}\cdot\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle=T_{\varepsilon}\cdot\varepsilon+T_{A}+T_{B}\cdot\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|,

where in the last line we plugin the definition of
T_{\varepsilon}
, and define
\mathtt{T}_{A}
and
\mathtt{T}_{B}
as the second term and the last term respectively. We bound them separately below:

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{A}
:
We note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}_{t}\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}\cdot\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}_{t}\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}\cdot\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon/2\}\cdot\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|

where the last line follows from Lemma
12
(when invoked with
\phi(z)=z
for which
\gamma=1
) and because
\widehat{y}_{t}\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})
.
Using the fact that
\mathbf{1}\{a\geq b\}\leq\nicefrac{{a}}{{b}}
for all
a,b\geq 0
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}\leq 4\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\frac{\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon}.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{B}
:
Fix any
t\leq T
, and first note that Lemma
24
implies that
\sum_{s=1}^{t}\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}
, and thus
{f^{\star}}
satisfies the constraint in the definition of
\Delta_{t}
in
(
5
)
and we must have that

\displaystyle\|{f^{\star}}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x_{t})\|\leq\Delta_{t}(x_{t}).

(54)

Plugging in the definition of
Z_{t}
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t}))>2\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\widehat{y}_{t}\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\|f_{t}(x_{t})-{f^{\star}}(x_{t})\|>\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\},

where the second inequality is due Lemma
12
(where we set
\gamma=1
for the square loss
\phi(z)=z
).
However, note that the term inside the indicator contradicts
(
54
)
(which always holds). Thus,

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}

\displaystyle=0.

Gathering the two bounds above, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon T_{\varepsilon}+8\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}(x_{t})-{f^{\star}}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{8}{\varepsilon}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)},

where the last inequality is due to Lemma
24
.

Since
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above, the final bound follows by choosing the best parameter
\varepsilon
, and by plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}
.

C.5.3
Total Number of Queries

Let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. Repeating the steps in the proof of Theorem
1
till
(
29
)
, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle=T_{\varepsilon}+\mathtt{T}_{D}+\mathtt{T}_{E},

where in the last line we used the definition of
T_{\varepsilon}
, and defined
\mathtt{T}_{D}
and
\mathtt{T}_{E}
respectively. We bound them separately below.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{D}
.

Recall
(
54
)
which implies that
{f^{\star}}
satisfies the bound
\|f_{t}(x_{t})-{f^{\star}}(x_{t})\|\leq\Delta_{t}(x_{t})
. Thus, using Lemma
13
(with
\gamma=1
for the link function
\phi(z)=z
), we get that

\displaystyle\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq 2\|f_{t}(x_{t})-{f^{\star}}(x_{t})\|+\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\Delta_{t}(x_{t})+\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t})).

The above implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{D}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))\leq 4\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle\leq 0,

where the last line follows from the fact that all the conditions inside the indictor can not hold simultaneously.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{E}
.
We note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t}(x_{t}))\leq 2\Delta_{t}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}(x_{t}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t}(x_{t})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\geq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{320{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}};{f^{\star}}).

where the last line follows from setting
\zeta=\varepsilon/4
in Lemma
25
.

Gathering the bounds above, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle\leq T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{640{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}};{f^{\star}}).

Since
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above, the final bound follows by choosing the best parameter
\varepsilon
, and by plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi_{\delta}(\mathcal{F},T)}
.

C.6
Proofs for Lower Bounds in Section
3.2

The proof of Theorem
3
below follows along the lines of the proof of the lower bound in Theorem 28 of
Foster et al. 2020
with minor changes.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Let
\beta\leq\zeta/2
be the largest number such that
\beta^{2}\leq\min\{\zeta^{2}/\mathfrak{s}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{F},\zeta,\beta),\zeta^{2}/16\}
. Given the function class
\mathcal{F}
, assume that
m=\mathfrak{s}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{F},\zeta,\beta)>0
(the lower bound is obvious when the star number is
0
). Let the target function
f^{\star}
, the data sequence
x^{1},\ldots,x^{m}
, and the function
f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}\in\mathcal{F}
be the witnesses for the fact that the star number is
m
(see Definition
3
). First, for any
i\in[m]
, we have that
|f^{\star}(x^{i})|>\zeta
by definition of the star number. Next note that, for each
f_{i}
, we have that, for any
j\neq i
,

\displaystyle|f_{i}(x^{j})|\geq|f^{\star}(x^{j})|-|f_{i}(x^{j})-f^{\star}(x^{j})|\geq\zeta-\beta\geq\zeta/2

On the other hand, from our definition of star number we have that,

|f_{i}(x^{i})|\geq\zeta/2.

Hence we are guaranteed that each
f_{i}
has a margin of at least
\zeta/2
on
x^{1},\ldots,x^{m}
. Now consider the distribution
\mu
over the context to be the uniform distribution over
\{x^{1},\ldots,x^{m}\}
. Also let
P^{i}(y=1\mid x)=\nicefrac{{1+f_{i}(x)}}{{2}}
be the conditional probability of label given context
x
. Let
D_{i}
denote the joint distribution over
\mathcal{X}\times\{\pm 1\}
given by drawing
x
’s from
\mu
and labels from
P^{i}
. Additionally, let
D_{0}
be given by drawing
x
’s from
\mu
and
y
conditioned on
x
as
P(y=1\mid x)=\nicefrac{{1+f^{\star}(x)}}{{2}}
. Finally, let
\nu=0
with probability
1/2
and
\nu\sim\mathrm{Uniform}([m])
with probability
1/2
. Note that by our premise,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\right]+\frac{1}{2m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left[\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\left[\mathbb{E}_{D_{\nu}}\left[\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\right]\right]\leq c\frac{\zeta T}{m},

(55)

where the value of the constant
c
will be set later. Furthermore, let
p_{t}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\Delta(\mathcal{Y})
denote the distribution over the label chosen by the given algorithm at round
t
given the history
\left(x_{1},y_{1},\dots,x_{t-1},y_{t-1}\right)
. We note that for any
i\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left[\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\zeta}{2}\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{y}\sim p_{t}(x)}\left[\mathbf{1}\{f_{i}(x)\hat{y}<0\}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{T\zeta}{2}\mathbb{E}_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}\En_{\hat{y}\sim p_{t}(x)}\mathbf{1}\{\text{\rm sign}(f_{i}(x))\neq\hat{y}\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T\zeta}{4}\mathbb{E}_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}p_{t}-\text{\rm sign}(f_{i})\right\|_{L_{1}(\mu)}

where in the first inequality we used that
f_{i}
has a margin of at least
\zeta/2
for any
x\in\{x^{1},\dots,x^{m}\}
as shown above, and the last inequality simply follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\lvert\En_{\hat{y}\sim p_{t}(x)}\mathbf{1}\{\text{\rm sign}(f_{i}(x))\neq\hat{y}\}\rvert=\lvert\En_{\hat{y}\sim p_{t}(x)}\left(\mathbf{1}\{\text{\rm sign}(f_{i}(x))=1\}-\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}=0\}\right)\rvert=\frac{1}{2}\lvert p_{t}(x)-f_{i}(x)\rvert,

and via an application of the Jensen’s inequality. Using the property that for any distribution with margin at least
\nicefrac{{\zeta}}{{2}}
, the algorithm satisfies
\En_{D_{i}}\left[\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\right]\leq\nicefrac{{c\zeta T}}{{m}}
, the above implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}p_{t}-\text{\rm sign}(f_{i})\right\|_{L_{1}(\mu)}\leq\frac{8c}{m},

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}p_{t}-\text{\rm sign}(f_{i})\right\|_{L_{1}(\mu)}\leq\frac{8c}{m}.

(56)

Hence, setting
c=64
and using Markov’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Pr_{{}_{D_{i}}}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}p_{t}-\text{\rm sign}(f_{i})\right\|_{L_{1}(\mu)}>\frac{2}{m}\right)\leq\frac{1}{16}.

Further note that
\left\|\text{\rm sign}(f_{j})-\text{\rm sign}(f_{i})\right\|_{L_{1}(\mu)}>\frac{4}{m}
and thus, condition on the fact that
\nu
is not equal to
0
, we can can identify
\nu
correctly with probability at least
1-1/16
. Thus, considering the reference measure
D_{0}
and using Fano’s inequality, we must have that

\log(2)\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathrm{KL}{\left(D_{0}\|D_{i}\right)}

Now we are left with bounding
\mathrm{KL}{\left(D_{0}\|D_{i}\right)}
. To this end, we first make a simple observation that the distribution on the
x_{t}
’s is the same under
D_{0}
and
D_{i}
. Hence on rounds
t
where
Z_{t}=0
we do not query for the labels in these rounds, and thus we do not glean any new information to distinguish
D_{i}
from
D_{0}
. In other words, we only need to consider rounds when
Z_{t}=1
. Hence we have:

\mathrm{KL}{\left(D_{0}\|D_{i}\right)}=\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\cdot\mathrm{kl}{\left(P_{0}(y_{t}=1|x_{t})\|P_{i}(y_{t}=1|x_{t})\right)}\right]

Assuming
\zeta>1/4
and using the bound on KL between Bernoulli variables we get,

\mathrm{KL}{\left(D_{0}\|D_{i}\right)}\leq\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[32N_{i}\zeta^{2}+8\left(\max_{j\neq i}N_{j}\right)\beta^{2}\right]

where
N_{i}=|\{t:Z_{t}=1,x_{t}=x^{i}\}
, and we used item-
\boldsymbol{(3)}
in the definition of star number for the
\zeta^{2}
term and item-
\boldsymbol{(1)}
for the
\beta^{2}
term. Hence we have that,

\displaystyle\log(2)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[32N_{i}\zeta^{2}+8\left(\max_{j\neq i}N_{j}\right)\beta^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32}{m}\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}N_{i}\right]\zeta^{2}+8\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[\max_{j}N_{j}\right]\beta^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32}{m}\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[N_{T}\right]\zeta^{2}+8\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[N_{T}\right]\beta^{2}

Since
\beta^{2}\leq\zeta^{2}/m
, we have that

\displaystyle\log(2)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{40}{m}\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[N_{T}\right]\zeta^{2}

Hence we conclude that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{D_{0}}\left[N_{T}\right]\geq\frac{\log(2)m}{40\zeta^{2}}

which yields the desired lower bound.
∎

Proof of Corollary
2
.

Consider the function class
\mathcal{F}=\{f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{\sqrt{T}}\}
where
f_{0}(x_{i})=1/2+\zeta
for every
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\sqrt{T}}
, and where
f_{i}(x_{i})=1/2-\zeta
and
f_{i}(x_{j})=1/2+\zeta
for any
j\neq i
. Note that selecting
f^{\star}=f_{0}
and
f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}
on
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\sqrt{T}}
, we can show that the star number
\mathfrak{s}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{F},1/8,1/2)=O(\sqrt{T})
(the disagreement coefficient of
\mathcal{F}
is also
O(\sqrt{T})
). Thus, using the converse of Theorem
3
we get that if the number of queries is smaller than
\sqrt{T}
, then there must exist some data distribution under which the regret bound of the algorithm is larger that
\sqrt{T}
.
∎

Appendix D
Imitation Learning: Learning from Single Expert

D.1
Imitation Learning Tools

We first recall useful additional notation. Recall that a policy
\pi
is a mapping from the states
\mathcal{X}
to actions
\mathcal{A}
. For any
h\leq H
, and random variable
\mathsf{Z}(x_{h},a_{h})
, we use the notation
\En_{\pi}\left[\mathsf{Z}(x_{h},a_{h})\right]
to denote the expectation w.r.t. trajectories
\{x_{1},a_{1}\dots,x_{H},a_{H}\}
sampled using the policy
\pi
.

The following lemma is the standard performance difference lemma, well known in the imitation learning and reinforcement learning literature.

Lemma 26

(Performance Difference Lemma;
Kakade and Langford 2002
;
Ross and Bagnell 2014
)
.

For any MDP
M
, and any two arbitrary stationary policies
\pi
and
\widetilde{\pi}
, we have

\displaystyle V^{\pi}-V^{\widetilde{\pi}}=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{x_{h},a_{h}\sim d^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{h}}\left[-A_{h}^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})\right],

where
A^{\pi}
is the advantage function of the policy
\pi
in MDP
M
, i.e.,
A_{h}^{\pi}(x,a)=Q_{h}^{\pi}(x,a)-V_{h}^{\pi}(x)
.

D.2
Proof of Proposition
1

MDP construction.

The underlying MDP is a binary tree of depth
H
. In particular, we construct the deterministic MDP
M=(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A},P,r,x_{1})
where state space
\mathcal{X}=\cup_{h=1}^{H}\mathcal{X}_{h}
with
\mathcal{X}_{h}=\{x_{h,i}\}_{i=1}^{2^{h-1}}
(we assume that
x_{1}=x_{1,1}
), action space
\mathcal{A}=\{0,1\}
, reward
r
is such that
r(x,a)=\mathrm{Bern}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\mathbf{1}\{x=x^{\star}\}\right)
for some special state
x^{\star}\in\mathcal{X}_{H}
. The transition dynamics
P
is deterministic and defines a binary tree over
\mathcal{X}
, i.e. for any
h
and
x_{h,i}
,
P(x^{\prime}\mid x_{h,i},a)=1
if
x^{\prime}=x_{h+1,2i-1}
and
a=0
, or
x^{\prime}=x_{h+1,2i+1}
and
a=1
, else
P(x^{\prime}\mid x,a)=0
.

We next define the expert policy
{\pi^{\star}}
, expert model
{f^{\star}}
and the class
\mathcal{F}
.
First, for any path
\tau=(x_{1},a_{1},\dots,x_{H},a_{H})
from the root state
x_{1}
to a terminal state
x_{H}
at the layer
H
, define the policy
\pi_{\tau}
as

\displaystyle\pi_{\tau}(x_{h})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a_{h}&\text{if}\quad(x_{h},a_{h})\in\tau\\
\bar{a}_{h}\leftarrow\text{Uniform}(\{0,1\})&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

In particular,
\pi_{\tau}
is defined such that for any state on the path
\tau
, we choose the corresponding action in
\tau
, and for any state outside of
\tau
, we choose an arbitrary (deterministic) action. Let
\mathcal{T}
denote the set of all
2^{H}
many paths from the root note
x_{1}
to a leaf node
x_{H}\in\mathcal{X}_{H}
. We define the class
\Pi=\{\pi_{\tau}\mid\tau\in\mathcal{T}\}
, and
\mathcal{F}=\{f_{\tau}\mid\tau\in\mathcal{T}\}
, where for any
\tau
, we define
f_{\tau}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{2}
as

\displaystyle f_{\tau}(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}})&\text{if}\qquad\pi_{\tau}(x)=0\\
(\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}},\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})&\text{if}\qquad\pi_{\tau}(x)=1\\
\end{cases}.

Next, let
\tau^{\star}=(x_{1},a^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2},\dots,x^{\prime}_{H-1},a^{\prime}_{H-1},x^{\star})
be the path from the root
x_{1}
to the special state
x^{\star}\in\mathcal{X}_{H}
on the underlying binary tree. We finally define
{\pi^{\star}}=\pi_{\tau^{\star}}
and
{f^{\star}}=f_{\tau^{\star}}
.

Lower bound.

Given the MDP construction, the class
\mathcal{F}
,
{f^{\star}}
and
{\pi^{\star}}
above, we now proceed to the desired lower bound for non-interactive imitation learning. First, note that
{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\argmax_{a}({f^{\star}}(x)[a])
for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
. Furthermore,
\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}}((x))=\frac{1}{2}\lvert f(x)[0]-f(x)[1]\rvert=\frac{1}{4}
for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
. Thus, for any
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{4}
,
T_{\varepsilon,h}=0
.

Next, for any policy
\pi
, note that
V^{\pi}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\mathbf{1}\{\pi={\pi^{\star}}\}
. Thus,
{\pi^{\star}}
is the unique
1/8
-suboptimal policy. Additionally, consider a noisy expert that draws its label according to
(
2
)
with the link function
\phi(z)=z
, i.e. on the state
x
, the expert draws its label from
a\sim f^{\star}(x)
. Now, suppose that the learner is given a dataset
\mathcal{D}
of
m
many trajectories drawn this noisy expert. There are two scenarios: either
\mathcal{D}
does not contain
\tau^{*}
, or
\mathcal{D}
contains the trajectory
\tau^{*}
.

\bullet

In the first case, the learner is restricted to finding
{\pi^{\star}}
by eliminating all other
\pi\neq{\pi^{\star}}
using the observations
\mathcal{D}
. Since,
\lvert\Pi\rvert=2^{H}
and each policy in the class is associated with a different path on the tree, we must have that
m=O(2^{H})
.

\bullet

In the second case, we need
\tau^{\star}\in\mathcal{D}
. However, note that probability of observing the trajectory
\tau^{\star}
when following the actions proposed by the noisy expert is
\Pr(\tau^{\star}\mid a_{h}\sim{f^{\star}}(x_{h}))=\left({3}/{4}\right)^{H}
. Thus, in order to observe
\tau^{\star}
with probability at least
3/4
in the dataset
\mathcal{D}
, we need
m=O((4/3)^{H})
.

In both the scenarios above, we need to collect exponentially many samples.

D.3
Proof of Theorem
6

Before delving into the proof, we recall the relevant notation. In Algorithm
3
, for any
h\leq H
,

\bullet

The label
y_{t,h}\sim\phi({f^{\star}}(x_{t,h}))
, , where
\phi
denotes the link-function given in
(
2
)
.

\bullet

The function
\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))=\argmax_{k}\phi(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))[k]
.

\bullet

For any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
, the margin is given by the gap between the value at the largest and the second largest coordinate (under the link function
\phi
), i.e.

\displaystyle\mathtt{Margin}(v)=\phi(v)[k^{\star}]-\max_{k\neq k^{\star}}\phi(v)[k],

where
k^{\star}\in\argmax_{k\in[K]}\phi(v)[k]
.

\bullet

We define
T_{\varepsilon}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))\leq\varepsilon\}
to denote the number of samples within
T
rounds of interaction for which the margin w.r.t.
{f^{\star}_{h}}
is smaller than
\varepsilon
.

\bullet

The trajectory at round
t
is generated using the dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}
to determine the states that the learner observes, starting from the state
x_{1}
.

\bullet

At round
t
, the learner collects data using the policy
\pi_{t}
such that at time
h
, and state
x
, the action
\pi_{t}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))
.

\bullet

For any policy
\pi
, let
\tau^{\pi}_{t}
denote the (counterfactual) trajectory that one would obtain by running
\pi
on the deterministic dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}
with the start state
x_{t,1}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\tau^{\pi}_{t}=\left\{x_{t,1}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,1}^{\pi}),\dots,x_{t,H}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,H}^{\pi})\right\}

(57)

where
x_{t,1}^{\pi}=x_{t,1}
and
x_{t,h+1}^{\pi}=\mathbb{T}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,h}^{\pi}))
.

\bullet

For a trajectory
\tau=\{x_{1},a_{1},\dots,x_{H},a_{H}\}
, we define the total return

\displaystyle R(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(x_{h},a_{h}).

(58)

\bullet

Additionally, for any policy
\pi
and dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{h}\}_{h\leq H}
, we define the trajectory obtained by running the policy
\pi
as

\displaystyle\tau^{\pi}=\{x_{1}^{\pi},\pi_{1}(x_{1}^{\pi}),x_{2}^{\pi},\dots\}.

\bullet

We define the function
\mathrm{Gap}:\mathbb{R}^{K}\times[K]\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{+}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gap}(v,k)=\max_{k^{\prime}}\phi(v)[k^{\prime}]-\phi(v)[k],

(59)

to denote the gap between the largest and the
k
-th coordinate of
v
, and note that
\mathtt{Margin}(v)\leq\mathrm{Gap}(v,k)
for all
k\neq k^{\star}
(due to Lemma
9
).

D.3.1
Supporting Technical Results

We first define a useful technical lemma which allows us to bound the gap between the total returns for policies
{\pi_{1}}
and
{\pi_{2}}
, under the dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{h}\}_{h\leq H}
. Recall that for a policy
\pi
, we define the trajectory
\tau^{\pi}
under
\{\mathbb{T}_{h}\}_{h\leq H}
and the start state
x_{1}
as the trajectory
\{x_{1}^{\pi},\pi(x_{1}^{\pi}),\dots,x_{H}^{\pi},\pi(x_{H}^{\pi})\}
where
x^{\pi}_{1}=x_{1}
, and
x^{\pi}_{h+1}\leftarrow\mathbb{T}_{h}(x^{\pi}_{h},\pi(x^{\pi}_{h}))
.

Lemma 27
.

Let
\{\mathbb{T}_{h}\}_{h\leq H}
be a deterministic dynamics, and let
x_{1}
be the start state. Let
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
be any two deterministic policies, and let
\tau^{\pi_{1}}=\{x_{1}^{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1}(x_{1}^{\pi_{1}}),x_{2}^{\pi_{1}},\dots\}
and
\tau^{\pi_{2}}=\{x_{1}^{\pi_{2}},\pi_{1}(x_{1}^{\pi_{2}}),x_{2}^{\pi_{2}},\dots\}
be two trajectories drawn using
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
on
\{\mathbb{T}_{h}\}_{h\leq H}
with start state
x_{1}
. Then, for any set
\mathtt{X}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, the total trajectory rewards satisfy

\displaystyle R(\tau^{\pi_{1}})-R(\tau^{\pi_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{h}^{\pi_{1}}\in\mathtt{X}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{2}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}})\neq\pi_{1}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}),x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}\notin\mathtt{X}\}.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{h}\leq H
denote the first timestep at which the policies
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
choose different actions under
\{\mathbb{T}_{h}\}_{h\leq H}
. Since the trajectories
\tau^{\pi_{1}}=\{x_{1}^{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1}(x_{1}^{\pi_{1}}),x_{2}^{\pi_{1}},\dots\}
and
\tau^{\pi_{2}}=\{x_{1}^{\pi_{2}},\pi_{1}(x_{1}^{\pi_{2}}),x_{2}^{\pi_{2}},\dots\}
are obtained by evolving through (the deterministic dynamics)
\{\mathbb{T}_{h}\}_{h\leq H}
using policies
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
respectively, and with the same state state
x_{1}
, we have that

\displaystyle x_{h}^{\pi_{1}}=x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for all $h\leq\mathfrak{h}$},

and

\displaystyle\pi_{1}(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}})=\pi_{2}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}})\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for all $h\leq\mathfrak{h}-1$}.

(60)

Starting from the definition of the cumulative reward
R(\cdot)
, we have that

\displaystyle R(\tau^{\pi_{1}})-R(\tau^{\pi_{2}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(r(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1}(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}}))-r(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}},\pi_{2}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\mathfrak{h}-1}\left(r(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1}(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}}))-r(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}},\pi_{2}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}))\right)+\sum_{h=\mathfrak{h}}^{H}\left(r(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1}(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}}))-r(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}},\pi_{2}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=\mathfrak{h}}^{H}\left(r(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1}(x_{h}^{\pi_{1}}))-r(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}},\pi_{2}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}))\right),

where the last line uses the fact that the trajectories (and thus the rewards)
\tau^{\pi_{1}}
and
\tau^{\pi_{2}}
are identical for the first
\mathfrak{h}-1
states and actions (see
(
60
)
). Since
(
60
)
also implies that
x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{1}}=x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{2}}
, for the ease of notation we define
x_{\mathfrak{h}}=x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{1}}=x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{2}}
. Using the fact that
\lvert r(x,a)\rvert\leq 1
and that
\pi_{1}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\neq\pi_{2}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})
(by definition of
\mathfrak{h}
), we can bound the above as

\displaystyle R(\tau^{\pi_{1}})-R(\tau^{\pi_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq 2(H-\mathfrak{h}+1)\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{1}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\neq\pi_{2}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\}

\displaystyle\leq 2H\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{1}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\neq\pi_{2}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\}

\displaystyle=2H\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{1}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\neq\pi_{2}(x_{\mathfrak{h}}),x_{\mathfrak{h}}\in\mathtt{X}\}+2H\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{1}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\neq\pi_{2}(x_{\mathfrak{h}}),x_{\mathfrak{h}}\notin\mathtt{X}\}

\displaystyle\leq 2H\mathbf{1}\{x_{\mathfrak{h}}\in\mathtt{X}\}+2H\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{1}(x_{\mathfrak{h}})\neq\pi_{2}(x_{\mathfrak{h}}),x_{\mathfrak{h}}\notin\mathtt{X}\}

\displaystyle=2H\mathbf{1}\{x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{1}}\in\mathtt{X}\}+2H\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{2}(x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{2}})\neq\pi_{1}(x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{2}}),x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{2}}\notin\mathtt{X}\}

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{h}^{\pi_{1}}\in\mathtt{X}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\pi_{2}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}})\neq\pi_{1}(x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}),x_{h}^{\pi_{2}}\notin\mathtt{X}\},

where the equality in second last line plugs in the fact that
x_{\mathfrak{h}}=x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{1}}=x_{\mathfrak{h}}^{\pi_{2}}
, and the last inequality is a straightforward upper bound.
∎

We will also be using Lemma
9
and Lemma
13
from Appendix
C.1
for bounding the
\mathtt{Margin}
in the regret bound proofs. Finally, we note the following properties of the function
{f^{\star}_{h}}
.

Lemma 28
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, the function
{f^{\star}_{h}}
satisfies for any
h\leq H
and
t\leq T
,

(a)

\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s,h}\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{s,h})-f_{s,h}(x_{s,h})\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)},

(b)

\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|\leq\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)}=\frac{4}{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}_{h};T)}+\frac{112}{\lambda^{2}}\log(4H\log^{2}(T)/\delta)
.

Proof.

(a)

We first note that we do not query oracle when
Z_{s,h}=0
, and thus we can ignore the time steps for which
Z_{s,h}=0
. Hence, for each
h\in[H]
, applying Lemma
5
along with the fact that
\sup_{x,f\in\mathcal{F}_{h}}\lvert f(x)\rvert\leq 1
yields

\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s,h}\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{s,h})-f_{s,h}(x_{s,h})\|^{2}\leq\frac{4}{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}_{h};T)}+\frac{112}{\lambda^{2}}\log(4\log^{2}(T)/\delta)

for all
t\leq T
. Then, we take the union bound for all
h\in[H]
, which completes the proof.

(b)

The second part follows from using the observation in part-(a) that
{f^{\star}_{h}}
satisfies the constraint in the definition of
\Delta_{t,h}
given in
(
12
)
, and thus

\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|\leq\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})
.

∎

The next technical lemma bounds the number of times when
\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\geq\zeta
and we query the expert. Note that Lemma
29
holds even if the sequence
\{x_{t,h}\}_{t\leq T}
was adversarially generated.

Lemma 29
.

Let
{f^{\star}}
satisfy Lemma
28
, and let
\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t})
be defined in
(
12
)
. Suppose we run Algorithm
3
on data sequence
\{\{x_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}\}_{t\leq T}
, and let
Z_{t,h}
be as defined in line
10
. Then, for any
\zeta>0
, with probability at least
1-H\delta
, for any
h\leq H
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\geq\zeta\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)}}{\zeta^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}_{h},\frac{\zeta}{2};{f^{\star}_{h}}),

where
\mathfrak{E}
denotes the eluder dimension is given in Definition
1
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of
Lemma
11
by replacing all
\lvert\cdot\rvert
with
\|\cdot\|
, and substitute the corresponding bounds for
{f^{\star}_{h}}
via Lemma
28
(instead of using Lemma
10
). We skip the proof for conciseness.
∎

D.3.2
Regret Bound

Recall that the trajectory at round
t
is generated using the dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}
. Define the policy
{\pi_{t}}
and
{\pi^{\star}}
such that for any
h\leq H
and
x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}
,

\displaystyle{\pi_{t}}(x_{h})=\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t,h}(x_{h})),\quad\text{and,}\quad{\pi^{\star}}(x_{h})=\mathtt{SelectAction}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{h})).

(61)

Furthermore, for any policy
\pi
, let
\tau^{\pi}_{t}
denote the trajectory that one would obtain by running
\pi
on the deterministic dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}
with the start state
x_{t,1}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\tau^{\pi}_{t}=\left\{x_{t,1}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,1}^{\pi}),\dots,x_{t,H}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,H}^{\pi})\right\}

(62)

where
x_{t,1}^{\pi}=x_{t,1}
and
x_{t,h+1}^{\pi}=\mathbb{T}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,h}^{\pi}))
. Note that Algorithm
3
collects trajectories using the policy
{\pi_{t}}
at round
t
. Thus, we have that

\displaystyle x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}=x_{t,h},

(63)

where
x_{t,h}
denotes the state at time step
h
in round
t
of Algorithm
3
. Finally, let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. We now have all the notation to proceed to the proof on our regret bound.

Step 1: Bounding the difference in return at round
t
.
Fix any
t\leq T
, and let
\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}}
and
\tau_{t}^{\pi^{\star}}
denote the trajectories that would have been sampled using the policies
{\pi_{t}}
and the policy
{\pi^{\star}}
at round
t
. Furthermore, define the set
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
as

\displaystyle\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon={}\bigcup_{h=1}^{H}\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}\mid{}\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x))\leq\varepsilon\}

(64)

Using Lemma
27
for the policies
{\pi_{t}}
and
{\pi^{\star}}
, and the set
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
defined above, we get that
9
9

9

The key advantage of using Lemma
27
is that the first term
\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}
accounts for the number steps at which a counterfactual trajectory sampled using
{\pi^{\star}}
goes to the state space with margin less than
\varepsilon
. Thus, we only pay for the number of times when the comparator policy
{\pi^{\star}}
would go to states with
\varepsilon
-margin (instead of when
{\pi_{t}}
does to such states).

\displaystyle R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi^{\star}}})-R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}})

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}),x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

(65)

\displaystyle=2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.08621pt+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.08621pt+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle=2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\mathtt{T}_{A}+2H\mathtt{T}_{B},

(66)

where the second line is obtained by plugging in
(
63
)
and the last line simply defines
\mathtt{T}_{A}
and
\mathtt{T}_{B}
to be the second and the third terms in the previous line without the
2H
multiplicative factor.

We bound
\mathtt{T}_{A}
and
\mathtt{T}_{B}
separately below.

\bullet

Bound on term
\mathtt{T}_{A}
.
Using the definition of
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
from
(
64
)
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))>\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h}),\phi({f^{\star}_{h}}({x_{t,h}}))[{\pi^{\star}}({x_{t,h}})]-\phi({f^{\star}_{h}}({x_{t,h}}))[{\pi_{t}}({x_{t,h}})]\geq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{\phi({f^{\star}_{h}}({x_{t,h}}))[{\pi^{\star}}({x_{t,h}})]-\phi({f^{\star}_{h}}({x_{t,h}}))[{\pi_{t}}({x_{t,h}})]\geq\varepsilon\},

where in the second last line we used the definition of
\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))
along with the fact that
{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})
. Using the relation in Lemma
12
for the term inside the indicator, we can further bound the above as

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{2\gamma\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|\geq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|^{2},

where in the second inequality we used:
\mathbf{1}\{a\geq b\}\leq\nicefrac{{a^{2}}}{{b^{2}}}
for any
a,b\geq 0
.

\bullet

Bound on term
\mathtt{T}_{B}
.
Before delving into the proof, first note that Lemma
28
-

(
b
)

implies that

\displaystyle\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|\leq\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h}).

(67)

Next, note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))>2\gamma\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\},

where in the last line we just plugged in the query condition under which
Z_{t,h}=0
. However note that the above two conditions inside the indicator imply that

\displaystyle 2\gamma\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})

\displaystyle<\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))

\displaystyle\leq\phi(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))[\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})]-\phi(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))[{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})]

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma\|f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|,

where the second line uses the definition of
\mathtt{Margin}(\cdot)
and the fact that
{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})
, the last line is due to Lemma
12
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\|f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|>\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\},

but the conditions inside the indicator in the above contradicts
(
67
)
(which holds with probability
1-\delta
). Thus, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}=0.

(68)

Plugging in the bounds on
\mathtt{T}_{A}
and
\mathtt{T}_{B}
in
(
66
)
, we get that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi^{\star}}})-R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}})

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+\frac{8H\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|^{2}.

(69)

Step 2: Bound on total regret.
Using the bound in
(
69
)
for each round
t
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi^{\star}}})-R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+\frac{8H\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h}\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{8H\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)},

(70)

where in the last line we use the definition of
T_{\varepsilon,h}
, and plug in the bound in Lemma
28
. Recall that
T_{\varepsilon,h}
denotes the number of times when the comparator policy
{\pi^{\star}}
enters the region
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
of states of small expert margin. Using the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)}
and ignoring
\log
factors and constants, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{H\gamma^{2}}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}_{h};T)}+\frac{H\gamma^{2}}{\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\log(1/\delta)\right).

Since
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above, the final bound follows by taking
\inf
over all feasible
\varepsilon
.

D.3.3
Total Number of Queries

Let
N_{T}
denote the total number of expert queries made by the learner within
T
rounds of interaction (with
H
steps per round). For
t\leq T
, let
h_{t}
denote the first timestep at which
Z_{t,h_{t}}=1
at round
t
. Additionally, let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. Thus, we have that

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}

(71)

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon},\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.08621pt+H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon},\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})>\frac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma}\}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{T}_{C}+H\mathtt{T}_{D}+H\mathtt{T}_{E},

where
\mathtt{T}_{C}
,
\mathtt{T}_{D}
and
\mathtt{T}_{E}
are the first, second and the third term respectively in the previous line. We bound them separately below:

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{C}
. Fix any
t\leq T
, and note that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{C})_{t}

\displaystyle=HZ_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=HZ_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\forall h<h_{t}:{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})=\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt+HZ_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\exists h<h_{t}:{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}.

(72)

For the second term, note that

\displaystyle Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\exists h<h_{t}:{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{h_{t}}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{h_{t}}Z_{t,h_{t}}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}
