\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{h_{t}}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

where in second inequality above, we used the fact that
Z_{t,h}=0
(and thus
\bar{Z}_{t,h}=1
) for all
h\leq h_{t}
, by the definition of
h_{t}
. However note that the right hand side in the last inequality is equivalent to the term
\mathtt{T}_{B}
defined above (where sum is now till
h_{t}
instead of
H
). Thus, using the bound in
(
68
)
in the above, we immediately get that

\displaystyle Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\exists h<h_{t}:{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\}=0.

For the first term in
(
72
)
, using the condition that
{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})=\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})
for all
h\leq h_{t}
, we get that
x_{t,h}=x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h}
and thus

\displaystyle HZ_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\forall h\leq h_{t}:{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})=\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle\leq HZ_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\leq H\mathbf{1}\{x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}.

Gathering the two terms above, and plugging in the definition of
T_{\varepsilon,h}
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{C}\leq H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}=H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{D}
. Using the definition of the set
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
and
Z_{t,h}
, we note that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{D})_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon},\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))\leq 2\gamma\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma}\}

(73)

Recall that Lemma
28
implies that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|\leq\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),

using which with Lemma
13
implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))+2\gamma\|{f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|

\displaystyle\leq\mathtt{Margin}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))+2\gamma\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h}).

Using the above bound with the conditions in
(
73
)
implies that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{D})_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))\leq 4\gamma\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))>\varepsilon,\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))\leq\varepsilon,\mathtt{Margin}({f^{\star}_{h}}(x_{t,h}))>\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle=0,

where the last equality holds because the two conditions in the indicator in the previous line can never occur simultaneously.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{E}
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon},\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})>\frac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{\Delta_{t,h}(x_{t,h})>\frac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma}\}.

An application of Lemma
29
in the above for each
h\leq H
implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{320\gamma^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}_{h},\frac{\varepsilon}{8\gamma};{f^{\star}_{h}}).

Gathering the bound above, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{320H\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}_{h},\frac{\varepsilon}{8\gamma};{f^{\star}_{h}}).

Plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}_{h},T)}
and ignoring
\log
factors and constants, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{H\gamma^{2}}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}_{h};T)}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}_{h},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{8\gamma}};{f^{\star}_{h}})+\frac{H\gamma^{2}}{\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\log(1/\delta)\right).

Notice that
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above so the final bound follows by taking
\inf
over all feasible
\varepsilon
. s

D.4
Proof for the Stochastic Setting

Algorithm
3
considers arbitrary deterministic dynamics
\{\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}\}_{t\leq T}
. When the underlying dynamics
\mathscr{T}
is stochastic, we can simply simulate Algorithm
3
, where we set
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}=\mathscr{T}(\cdot;\iota_{t})
where
\iota_{t}
is drawn i.i.d. for every
t\leq T
. In the following, we provide regret and query complexity bounds for stochastic dynamics.

Regret bound.

Note that Theorem
6
bounds the difference of cumulative rewards of trajectories drawn using the policies
{\pi_{t}}
and
{\pi^{\star}}
on the adversarially chosen deterministic dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
respectively. In particular, we bound

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(R(\tau^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t})-R(\tau^{{\pi_{t}}}_{t})\right).

On the other hand, when the dynamics is stochastic, we aim to bound the gap between the expected values
V^{{\pi^{\star}}}-V^{{\pi_{t}}}
obtained under the stochastic dynamics
\mathscr{T}
. We obtain this bound by pushing all the stochasticity into the choice of random seed
\iota
. Fix any
t\leq T
, and consider the deterministic dynamics
(\mathbb{T}_{t,1},\dots,\mathbb{T}_{t,H})
obtained by setting the random seed to be
\iota_{t}
in the stochastic dynamics
\mathscr{T}
, i.e.
(\mathbb{T}_{t,1},\dots,\mathbb{T}_{t,H})\vcentcolon={}\mathscr{T}(\penalty\ ;\penalty\ \iota_{t})
. Thus, for any policy
\pi

\displaystyle V^{\pi}

\displaystyle=\En_{\iota_{t}}\left[R(\tau^{\pi}_{t})\mid(\mathbb{T}_{t,1},\dots,\mathbb{T}_{t,H})=\mathscr{T}(\penalty\ ;\penalty\ \iota_{t})\right].

In the following, we will bound the difference in the value function
V^{\pi}-V^{{\pi_{t}}}
, by appealing to the regret bound in the proof of Theorem
6
using appropriate concentration inequalities. First, recall that in Algorithm
3
, the dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}
is chosen before the round
t
, and that the policy
{\pi_{t}}
only depends on the interaction till round
t-1
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}V^{\pi}-V^{{\pi_{t}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{\iota_{t}}\left[R(\tau^{\pi}_{t})-R(\tau^{\pi^{\star}}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{\iota_{t}}\left[2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}),x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}\right],

where the last holds due to Lemma
27
and the set
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
is defined in
(
64
)
. An application of Lemma
4
in the above implies that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}V^{\pi}-V^{{\pi_{t}}}

\displaystyle\leq 4H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+4H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}),x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+32H^{2}\log(2/\delta).

The rest of the proof is identical to the proof of Theorem
6
from
(
65
)
onwards. They query complexity can be similarly computed.

Appendix E
Learning from Multiple Experts

We next provide the proofs for learning from multiple experts. We first consider the selective sampling setting, and provide the setup, relevant notation and our algorithm for selective sampling with multiple experts in Appendix
E.1
. We then move to the imitation learning with multiple experts.

E.1
Selective Sampling

1:

Parameters
\delta,\gamma,\lambda,T,M
, function classes
\{\mathcal{F}^{m}\}_{m\leq M}
, and online oracles
\{\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}^{m}\}_{m\leq M}
.

2:

Set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}=\frac{4}{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m};T)}+\frac{112}{\lambda^{2}}\log(4M\log^{2}(T)/\delta).

3:

Learner computes
f^{m}_{1}\leftarrow\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}^{m}_{1}(\emptyset)
for every
m\in[M]
.

4:

Learner defines
F_{t}(x_{t})=\left[f^{1}_{t}(x_{t}),\dots,f^{M}_{t}(x_{t})\right]
.

5:

for

t=1
to
T

do

6:

Nature chooses
x_{t}
.

7:

Learner plays the action
\widehat{y}_{t}=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t}(x_{t}))

8:

For every
m\in[M]
, learner computes

\displaystyle\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t})\vcentcolon={}\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{m}}\penalty\ \|f(x_{t})-f^{m}_{t}(x_{t})\|\quad\text{s.t.}\quad\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s}\left\|f(x_{s})-f^{m}_{s}(x_{s})\right\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)},

(74)

and sets
{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})=[\Delta^{1}_{t}(x_{t}),\dots,\Delta^{M}_{t}(x_{t})]
.

9:

Learner decides whether to query:
Z_{t}=\mathtt{Query}(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t}))
.

10:

if

Z_{t}=1

then

11:

for

m=1
to
M

do

12:

Learner queries the
m
-th expert for its action
y^{m}_{t}

13:

f^{m}_{t+1}\leftarrow\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}^{m}_{t}(\{x_{t},y^{m}_{t}\})
.

14:

else

15:

f^{m}_{t+1}\leftarrow f^{m}_{t}
for all
m\in[M]
.

Algorithm 6

S
elective S
A
mplin
G
with Qu
E
ries to
\boldsymbol{M}
Experts (
SAGE
-\mathsf{M}
)

We first discuss the setup and the relevant notation. The learner has access to feedback from
M
experts, each with their ground truth models
\{{f^{\star,m}}\}_{m\in[M]}
respectively. At round
t
, on the given context
x_{t}
, the learner plays the action
\widehat{y}_{t}\in[K]
. Additionally, the learner can choose to query the experts, and receive feedback
y_{t}^{m}\sim\phi({f^{\star,m}}(x_{t}))
for each expert
m\in[M]
. The learner suffers a loss if the chosen action does not match with the actions
\{y_{t}\}_{t\leq T}
sampled jointly using the noisy experts, e.g. we may have

\displaystyle y_{t}\sim\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x_{t}))\right),

(75)

where
F^{\star}(x_{t})=[f^{\star,1}(x_{t}),\dots,f^{\star,M}(x_{t})]
denotes the concatenation of the scores predicted by each expert, and
\mathscr{A}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{K}
denotes an aggregation function
(known to the learner) that maps the predictions of the estimated experts to distributions over actions. Some illustrative examples of
\mathscr{A}
are given in Section
5
under
(
14
)
. The goal of the learner is to minimize its regret w.r.t. the comparator policy
{\pi^{\star}}
that knows the expert models
F^{\star}
and is defined such that

\displaystyle{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\argmax_{k\in[K]}\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x_{t}))[k]\right).

(76)

Thus, we wish to bound

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{y}_{t}\neq y_{t}\}-\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\}.

(77)

Additionally, in Algorithm
6
, for any round
t\in[T]
, we have

\bullet

The function
\mathtt{SelectAction}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}\mapsto[K]
chooses the action to play at round
t
, and is defined as:

\displaystyle\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t}(x_{t}))=\argmax_{k}\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F_{t}(x_{t}))\right)[k].

(78)

\bullet

Each expert computes the width
\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t})
by solving
(
74
)
.

\bullet

Given the context
x_{t}
and the function
F_{t}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}
, the learner decided whether to query via
Z_{t}=\mathtt{Query}(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t}))
where we define the function
\mathtt{Query}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}\times\mathbb{R}^{M}
as

\displaystyle\mathtt{Query}(U;\vec\Delta)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon={}\sup_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}}\penalty\ \mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{SelectAction}(U)\neq\mathtt{SelectAction}(V)\}\quad

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\text{s.t.}\quad\|U[:,m]-V[:,m]\|_{2}\leq\vec\Delta[m]\qquad\forall m\leq M.

(79)

\bullet

For the ease of notation, given a
F(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}
, we define the set
\mathbb{B}_{\infty}(F(x),{\vec{\Delta}})
to denote the set of all matrices
F^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}
such that
\|F(x)[:,m]-F^{\prime}(x)[:,m]\|\leq{\vec{\Delta}}[m]
for all
m\leq M
.

Finally, our bounds depend on the notation of margin defined w.r.t.
\mathtt{Query}
. In particular, for a sequence of contexts
\{x_{s}\}_{s\leq t}
, we define

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}.

(80)

E.1.1
Supporting Technical Results

The following technical lemma is useful in analyzing the constraints in the computation of
\Delta^{m}_{t}
in Algorithm
6
.

Lemma 30
.

With probability at least
1-{}\delta
, for any
t\leq T
and
m\leq M
, the function
{f^{\star,m}}
satisfies

(a)

\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s}\|f^{\star,m}(x_{s})-f^{m}_{s}(x_{s})\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)},

(b)

\|f^{\star,m}(x_{t})-f^{m}_{t}(x_{t})\|\leq\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t})
,

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}=\frac{4}{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m};T)}+\frac{112}{\lambda^{2}}\log(4M\log^{2}(T)/\delta).

Proof.

(a)

The part-(a) follows from an application of Lemma
5
for each
m\in[M]
, where we note that we do not query oracle when
Z_{s}=0
, and thus do not count the time steps for which
Z_{s}=0
. Each expert
m\in[M]
can be bound independently, and then a union bound gives the desired result.

(b)

The second part follows from using the observation in part-(a) in
(
74
)
.

∎

For the rest of the proof, we condition on the
1-\delta
probability event under which Lemma
30
holds.

Lemma 31
.

Let
{f^{\star,m}}
satisfy Lemma
30
, and let
\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t})
be defined in Algorithm
6
. Suppose we run Algorithm
6
on data sequence
\{x_{t}\}_{t\leq 1}
, and let
Z_{t}
be as defined in line
9
. Then, for any
\zeta>0
, with probability at least
1-M\delta
, for any
m\in[M]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Delta_{t}(x_{t})\geq\zeta\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}}{\zeta^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m},\frac{\zeta}{2};{f^{\star,m}}),

where
\mathfrak{E}
denotes the eluder dimension given in Definition
1
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of Lemma
11
where we handle each
m\in[M]
separately, and substitute the corresponding bounds for
{f^{\star,m}}
via Lemma
30
(instead of using Lemma
10
). We skip the proof for conciseness.
∎

E.1.2
Regret Bound

Recall that the policy
{\pi_{t}}
is given by

{\pi_{t}}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t}(x_{t}))
. Furthermore, the comparator policy
{\pi^{\star}}
given in
(
76
)
is

{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F^{\star}(x))
.

In the following, we measure regret w.r.t. any arbitrary ground truth actions
\{y_{1},\dots,y_{T}\}
, i.e. we make no assumptions on the process that generates
\{y_{1},\dots,y_{T}\}
10
10

10

In Appendix
E.1.4
, we provide an improvement over the bounds in this section when
\{y_{1},\dots,y_{T}\}
are generated according to
(
75
)
, and when
\mathscr{A}
is
\eta
-Lipschitz.
. Starting from the definition of regret,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pr\left({\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)-\Pr\left({\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\}\cdot\left\lvert\Pr\left(y_{t}={\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\right)-\Pr\left(y_{t}={\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\}.

Let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. We can further decompose the above into the following three terms:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{Z}_{t}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{T}_{A}+\mathtt{T}_{B}+\mathtt{T}_{C},

where
\mathtt{T}_{A},\mathtt{T}_{B}
and
\mathtt{T}_{C}
are defined to be the first term, second term and the third term in the previous line respectively, which we bound separately below:

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{A}
:
Note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}=T_{\varepsilon},

where the last line uses the definition of
T_{\varepsilon}
.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{B}
:
Using the definition of the function
\mathtt{Query}
, we note that if
{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})
and
\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0
, then there must exists an
m\in[M]
for which

\displaystyle\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon.

(81)

Thus, we have that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{B})_{t}

\displaystyle\leq Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}

\displaystyle\leq Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\exists m\in[M]:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}},

(82)

where the second line plugs in
(
81
)
, and the last line uses the fact that
\mathbf{1}\{a\geq b\}\leq\nicefrac{{a^{2}}}{{b^{2}}}
for all
a,b\geq 0
.
Gathering the above bound for all
t\leq T
implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)},

where the last line follows from an application of Lemma
30
for each
m\in[M]
.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{C}
:
Recall that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{C}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{Z}_{t}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t}))=0,{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\},

(83)

where the last line just plugs in the condition under which
\bar{Z}_{t}=1
, and removes the condition
\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0
from the indicator. Next, note that for any
t\leq T
, an application of Lemma
30
implies that with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
m\in M
,

\displaystyle\left\|f^{\star,m}(x_{t})-f^{m}_{t}(x_{t})\right\|\leq\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t}).

(84)

However, if
\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t}))=0
then we must have that for all
F^{\prime}
that satisfy
\|F^{\prime}[:,m]-f^{m}_{t}(x_{t})\|\leq\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t})
for all
m\in[M]
, we must have that
\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t}(x_{t}))=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F^{\prime}(x_{t}))
. However, since
F^{\star}
satisfies
(
84
)
, we get that
\mathtt{SelectAction}(F^{\star}(x_{t}))

=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F^{\prime}(x_{t}))
or equivalently
{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})={\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})
. This contradicts the conditions in
(
83
)
, implying that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{C}

\displaystyle=0.

Gathering the bounds above, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\leq T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}.

Plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}
from Lemma
30
, and ignoring
\log(1/\delta)
factors and constants, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\lesssim T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m};T)}.

(85)

Since
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above, the final bound follows by choosing the best parameter
\varepsilon
.

E.1.3
Total Number of Queries

Let
N_{T}
denote the total number of expert queries made by the learner in
T
rounds of interactions, and let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. We have:

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1,\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1,\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1,\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1,\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0,\|{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\|_{\infty}\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1,\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0,\|{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\|_{\infty}>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}}\}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{T}_{\varepsilon}+\mathtt{T}_{D}+\mathtt{T}_{E},

where in the last line, we plug in the definition of
\mathtt{T}_{\varepsilon}
, and defined
\mathtt{T}_{D}
and
\mathtt{T}_{E}
as the second-last term and the last term respectively. We bound them separately below:

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{D}
.
Recall that the
t
-th term in
(T_{D})_{t}
is

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{D})_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1,\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0,\sup_{m\in[M]}\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}}\}.

In the following, we will show that for any
t\leq T
, all the conditions in the above indicator can not hold simultaneously. First note that if
\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t}(x_{t}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\right)=1
, then there exists an
\widetilde{F}
such that

\displaystyle\mathtt{SelectAction}(\widetilde{F}(x_{t})))\neq\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t}(x_{t}))

(86)

and

\displaystyle\forall m\in[M]:\qquad\|\widetilde{F}(x_{t})[:,m]-F_{t}(x_{t})[:,m]\|\leq\Delta_{t}^{m}(x_{t}).

(87)

On the other hand, recall Lemma
30
, which implies that

\displaystyle\forall m\in[M]:\qquad\|F^{\star}(x_{t})[:,m]-F_{t}(x_{t})[:,m]\|\leq\Delta_{t}^{m}(x_{t}).

(88)

Triangle inequality along with the bounds
(
87
)
and
(
88
)
implies that

\displaystyle\forall m\in[M]:\qquad\|F^{\star}(x_{t})[:,m]-\widetilde{F}(x_{t})[:,m]\|\leq 2\Delta_{t}^{m}(x_{t})<\varepsilon,

(89)

where the second inequality holds whenever
\sup_{m}\Delta_{t}^{m}(x_{t})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}}
.

However, note that
(
89
)
contradicts the fact that
\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0
since both
\widetilde{F}
and
F_{t}
satisfy the norm constraints in the definition of
\mathtt{Query}
, but we can not simultaneously have that

\displaystyle\mathtt{SelectAction}(F^{\star}(x_{t})))=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t}(x_{t}))=\mathtt{SelectAction}(\widetilde{F}(x_{t})),

due to
(
86
)
. This implies that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{D})_{t}

\displaystyle=0.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{E}
.
Again using the definition of
Z_{t}
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\|{\vec{\Delta}}_{t}(x_{t})\|_{\infty}>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\exists m\in[M]:\Delta^{m}(x_{t})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\Delta^{m}(x_{t})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}}\}

Using Lemma
34
to bound the term in the right hand side for each
m\in[M]
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{180{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}};{f^{\star,m}})

Gathering the bounds above, we have

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle\leq T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{180}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}};{f^{\star,m}}).

Plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}
, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m};T)}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{3}};{f^{\star,m}})+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\log(1/\delta)\right).

Since
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above, the final bound follows by choosing the best parameter
\varepsilon
.

E.1.4
Improved Bound when Aggregator
\mathscr{A}
is
\eta
-Lipschitz

In this section, we show that the
1/\varepsilon^{2}
term in the regret bound
(
85
)
can be improved under additional modeling assumptions. Firstly, we assume that corresponding to the contexts
\{x_{t}\}_{t\leq T}
, the labels
\{y_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
that we compare to in the regret definition in
(
77
)
are sampled according to
y_{t}\sim\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x_{t}))\right)
(See
(
75
)
for more details.). Secondly, we assume that the aggregator function
\mathscr{A}
that combines experts models is
\eta
-Lipschitz.

Assumption 2

(Lipschitzness)
.

For any
U,V\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}
, the function
\mathscr{A}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}\mapsto\Delta(K)
satisfies

\|\mathscr{A}(U)-\mathscr{A}(V)\|\leq\eta\|U-V\|_{F}.

We first provide a useful technical lemma that is crucial for our improved bound.

Lemma 32
.

Suppose that for any
x
, the labels
y
are generated from
y\sim\mathscr{A}(\phi(F^{\star}(x)))
. Then, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
and function
F\in\mathcal{F}^{M}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\Pr[y={\pi^{\star}}(x)]-\Pr[y=\pi(x)]\rvert\leq 2\eta\gamma\|F^{\star}(x)-F(x)\|_{{}_{F}}

where
\pi(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F(x))=\argmax_{k}\mathscr{A}(\phi(F(x))[k]
, and
{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F^{\star}(x))=\argmax_{k}\mathscr{A}(\phi(F^{\star}(x)))[k]
.

Proof.

Assume that
{\pi^{\star}}(x)\neq\pi(x)
. Recalling that
{\pi^{\star}}(x)
satisfies the condition
{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\argmax_{k}\mathscr{A}(F^{\star}(k))
, we get that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\Pr[y_{t}={\pi^{\star}}(x)]-\Pr[y_{t}=\pi(x)]

\displaystyle=\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x))\right)[{\pi^{\star}}(x)]-\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x))\right)[\pi(x)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x))\right)[{\pi^{\star}}(x)]-\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x))\right)[\pi(x)]+\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F(x))\right)[\pi(x)]-\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F(x))\right)[{\pi^{\star}}(x)]

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F^{\star}(x))\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F(x))\right)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\|F^{\star}(x)-F(x)\|_{{}_{F}},

where in the third line we used the fact that
\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F(x))\right)[\pi(x)]\geq\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F(x))\right)[{\pi^{\star}}(x)]
, and the last line holds because
\mathscr{A}
is
\eta
-Lipschitz and
\phi
is
\gamma
-Lipschitz.
∎

Improved Regret Bound.

Starting from the definition of regret, we note that:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pr\left({\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)-\Pr\left({\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\neq y_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\}\cdot\left\lvert\Pr\left(y_{t}={\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\right)-\Pr\left(y_{t}={\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})\}\cdot 2\eta\gamma\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F},

where the last line plugs in Lemma
32
. Let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. We can further decompose the above as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}\cdot 2\eta\gamma\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}\cdot 2\eta\gamma\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{Z}_{t}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}\cdot 2\eta\gamma\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{T}_{A}+\mathtt{T}_{B}+\mathtt{T}_{C},

(90)

where
\mathtt{T}_{A},\mathtt{T}_{B}
and
\mathtt{T}_{C}
are the first term, second term and the third term in the previous line, respectively. Using the identical analysis as in Appendix
E.1.2
, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}\leq T_{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{and,}\qquad\mathtt{T}_{C}=0.

We next provide the improved bound for
\mathtt{T}_{B}
. Using the definition of the function
\mathtt{Query}
, we note that if
{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t})
and
\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0
, then there must exists an
m\in[M]
for which

\displaystyle\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon.

(91)

Thus, considering only the
t
-th item in
\mathtt{T}_{B}
, we get that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{B})_{t}

\displaystyle\leq Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t}),\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}\cdot 2\eta\gamma\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\gamma Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\exists m\in[M]:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F}.

(92)

Let
\mathcal{M}_{t}\vcentcolon={}\{m\in[M]:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}
, and note that

\displaystyle\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{(M-\lvert\mathcal{M}_{t}\rvert)\varepsilon^{2}+\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{M}\varepsilon+\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|,

(93)

where the second line uses the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{t}
and the last line is due to subadditivity of
\sqrt{\cdot}
. Using the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{t}
and the bound
(
93
)
in
(
92
)
, we get that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{B})_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\gamma Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\forall m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\|F^{\star}(x_{t})-F_{t}(x_{t})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\gamma\sqrt{M}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\forall m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+2\eta\gamma\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\forall m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|.

For the first term in the above,

\displaystyle Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\forall m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\exists m\in[M]:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\varepsilon

where the last line follows by using the fact that
\mathbf{1}\{a\geq b\}\leq\frac{a^{2}}{b^{2}}
for all
a,b\geq 0
. For the second term,

\displaystyle\hskip-108.405pt\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\forall m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}:\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}Z_{t}\mathbf{1}\{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|>\varepsilon\}\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon},

where second last line uses that
\mathbf{1}\{a\geq b\}\leq\nicefrac{{a}}{{b}}
for all
a,b\geq 0
. Taking the two together, we get

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{B})_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\gamma\left(\sqrt{M}+1\right)\sum_{m=1}^{M}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq 4\eta\gamma\sqrt{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}Z_{t}\frac{\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon}.

(94)

The above implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\mathtt{T}_{B})_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\eta\gamma\sqrt{M}}{\varepsilon}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\|f_{t}^{m}(x_{t})-f^{\star,m}(x_{t})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\eta\gamma\sqrt{M}}{\varepsilon}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)},

where the last line plugs in the bound in Lemma
30
.

Using the above derived bounds on
\mathtt{T}_{A}
,
\mathtt{T}_{B}
and
\mathtt{T}_{C}
in
(
90
)
, we get that:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\leq T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{4\eta\gamma\sqrt{M}}{\varepsilon}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}.

Plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m},T)}
from Lemma
30
, and ignoring
\log
factors and constants, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\lesssim T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{4\eta\gamma\sqrt{M}}{\lambda\varepsilon}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m};T)}+\frac{4\eta\gamma M^{3/2}}{\lambda^{2}\varepsilon}\log(1/\delta).

We finally note that the regret bound in Appendix
E.1.2
also holds simultaneously (since it holds even when we do not make the assumption
2
). Thus,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\lesssim T_{\varepsilon}+\min\{\frac{1}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{4\eta\gamma\sqrt{M}}{\lambda\varepsilon}\}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m};T)}.

Notice that
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above so the final bound follows by taking
\inf
over all feasible
\varepsilon
. The query complexity bound remains identical to the one shown in Appendix
E.1.3
.

E.2
Imitation Learning (Proof of Theorem
7
)

We next proceed to imitation learning with multiple experts.

1:

Parameters
\delta,\gamma,\lambda,T
, function classes
\{\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h}\}_{h\leq H,m\leq M}
, online oracles
\{\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}^{m}_{h}\}_{h\leq H,m\leq M}
w.r.t.
\ell_{\phi}
.

2:

Set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}=\frac{4}{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h};T)}+\frac{112}{\lambda^{2}}\log(4MH\log^{2}(T)/\delta).

3:

Compute
f^{m}_{1,h}=\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}_{1,h}(\emptyset)
for each
h\in[H]
and
m\in[M]
.

4:

for

t=1
to
T

do

5:

Nature chooses the state
x_{t,1}
.

6:

for

h=1
to
H

do

7:

Define
F^{m}_{t,h}(x)\vcentcolon={}[f^{1}_{t,h}(x),\dots,f^{M}_{t,h}(x)]
.

8:

Learner plays
\widehat{y}_{t,h}=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))
.

9:

Learner transitions to the next state in this round
x_{t,h+1}\leftarrow\mathbb{T}_{t,h}(x_{t,h},\widehat{y}_{t,h})
.

10:

For each
m\in[M]
, learner computes

\displaystyle\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon={}\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h}}\penalty\ \|f(x_{t,h})-f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\quad\text{s.t.}\quad\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s,h}\left\|f(x_{s,h})-f^{m}_{s,h}(x_{s,h})\right\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}.

(95)

and defines
{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})=[\Delta^{1}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),\dots,\Delta^{M}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})]
.

11:

Learner decides whether to query:
Z_{t,h}=\mathtt{Query}(F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))

12:

if

Z_{t,h}=1

then

13:

for

m=1
to
M

do

14:

Learner queries expert
m
for its label
y^{m}_{t,h}

for
x_{t,h}
.

15:

f^{m}_{t+1,h}\leftarrow\text{$\mathsf{Oracle}$}^{m}_{t+1,h}(\{x_{t,h},y_{t,h}\})

16:

else

17:

f^{m}_{t+1,h}\leftarrow f^{m}_{t,h}
for each
m\in[M]
.

Algorithm 7

Inte
RA
cti
V
e
I
mitati
O
n
L
earning V
I
a Active Queries to
\boldsymbol{M}
Experts (
RAVIOLI
-\mathsf{M}
)

We first discuss the setup and the relevant notation. We are in the imitation learning setup introduced in
Appendix
D.3
. In particular, the learner interacts with
M
experts in
T
episodes/rounds, each consisting of
H
timesteps. For any
h\leq H
, each of the
M
experts have a ground truth model
{f^{\star,m}_{h}}
respectively. Given the context
x_{t,h}
for time step
h
in round
t
, the learner plays the actions
\widehat{y}_{t,h}\in[K]
, and can additionally choose to query the experts (by setting
Q_{t,h}=1
) to receive noisy feedback
y_{t,h}^{m}\sim\phi({f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t}))
from each expert
m\in[M]
. After playing the chosen action
\widehat{y}_{t,h}
, the learner then transitions to state
x_{t,h+1}\leftarrow\mathbb{T}_{t,h}(x_{t,h},\widehat{y}_{t,h})
where
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\in[H]}
is a sequence of deterministic dynamics (unknown to the learner).

The setup for imitation learning builds on the notation introduced for selective sampling with multiple experts in Appendix
E.1
. In particular, in Algorithm
7
, for any round
t\leq T
and
h\leq H
:

\bullet

The aggregation function
\mathscr{A}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{K}
,
known to the learner, maps the predictions of the estimated experts to distributions over actions. Some illustrative examples are given in Section
5
under
(
14
)
.

\bullet

The function
\mathtt{SelectAction}{}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}\mapsto[K]
chooses the action to play at round
t
, and is defined as:

\displaystyle\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))=\argmax_{k}\mathscr{A}\left(\phi(F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))\right)[k],

(96)

where
F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))=[f^{1}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})),\dots,f^{M}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))]
, and
\phi
denotes the link-function given in
(
2
)
.

\bullet

Our goal in Algorithm
7
is to compete with the policy
{\pi^{\star}}
defined such that for any
x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}
,

\displaystyle{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{h}^{\star}(x))

(97)

where
F_{h}^{\star}(x)=[f^{\star,1}_{h}(x),\dots,f^{\star,1}_{h}(x)]\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}
.

\bullet

Given the context
x_{t,h}
and the function
F_{t,h}:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}
, the learner decided whether to query via
Z_{t,h}=\mathtt{Query}(F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))
where we define the function
\mathtt{Query}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}\times\mathbb{R}^{M}
as

\displaystyle\mathtt{Query}(U;\vec\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon={}\sup_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times M}}\penalty\ \mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{SelectAction}(U)\neq\mathtt{SelectAction}(V)\}\quad

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\text{s.t.}\quad\|U[:,m]-V[:,m]\|_{2}\leq\vec\varepsilon[m]\qquad\forall m\leq M.

(98)

At round
t
, the learner interactions with transition dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}
and collects data. Without loss of generality, we assume that the learner always starts from the state
x_{t,1}
. We next recall the interaction at round
t
:

\bullet

The learner collects data using the policy
\pi_{t}
, defined such that

\displaystyle\pi_{t}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(f_{t,h}(x_{t,h})).

for any
h\leq H
, and state
x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}
.

\bullet

For any policy
\pi
, we use the notation
\tau^{\pi}_{t}
to denote the (counterfactual) trajectory that would have been generated by running
\pi
on the deterministic dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}
with the start state
x_{t,1}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\tau^{\pi}_{t}=\left\{x_{t,1}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,1}^{\pi}),\dots,x_{t,H}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,H}^{\pi})\right\},

(99)

where
x_{t,1}^{\pi}=x_{t,1}
and
x_{t,h+1}^{\pi}=\mathbb{T}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}^{\pi},\pi(x_{t,h}^{\pi}))
.

\bullet

For any trajectory
\tau=\{x_{1},a_{1},\dots,x_{H},a_{H}\}
, we define the total return

\displaystyle R(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(x_{h},a_{h}).

(100)

The goal of the learner is to minimize its regret which is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}R(\tau^{\pi^{\star}}_{t})-\sum_{t=1}^{T}R(\tau^{{\pi_{t}}}_{t}).

(101)

Finally, our bounds depend on the notation of margin defined w.r.t. the function
\mathtt{Query}
defined above. In particular, for a sequence of contexts
\{\{T_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}\}_{t\leq T}
, we define
T_{\varepsilon,h}
as

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon,h}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F_{h}^{\star}(x_{t,h}^{\pi^{\star}}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}.

(102)

In the above we count for the number of time steps when the counterfactual states
\{x_{t,h}^{\pi^{\star}}\}_{t\leq T}
, reached under the given dynamics if we had executed
{\pi^{\star}}
, are within the
\varepsilon
-margin region. Note that even though the observed states
\{\{x_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H}\}_{t\leq T}
are such that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F_{h}^{\star}(x_{t,h}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}
is large, we would not pay for this in our margin term
T_{\varepsilon,h}
.

E.2.1
Supporting Technical Results

Lemma 33
.

With probability at least
1-{}\delta
, for any
m\leq M
, and
t\leq T
and
h\leq H
, the function
{f^{\star,m}}
satisfies

(a)

\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s,h}\|{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{s,h})-f^{m}_{s,h}(x_{s,h})\|^{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}
,

(b)

\|{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|\leq\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})
,

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}=\frac{4}{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h};T)}+\frac{112}{\lambda^{2}}\log(4MH\log^{2}(T)/\delta).

Proof.

(a)

We first note that we do not query oracle when
Z_{s,h}=0
, and thus we can ignore the time steps for which
Z_{s,h}=0
. Hence, for each
h\in[H]
and
m\in[M]
, applying Lemma
5
yields

\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}Z_{s,h}\|{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{s,h})-f_{s,h}(x_{s,h})\|^{2}\leq\frac{4}{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h};T)}+\frac{112}{\lambda^{2}}\log(4\log^{2}(T)/\delta)

for all
t\leq T
. Then, we take the union bound for all
h\in[H]
and
m\in[M]
, which completes the proof.

(b)

The second part follows from using part-(a) along with the definition in
(
95
)
.

∎

The next lemma bound the number of times when
\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\geq\zeta
, and we query. Note that Lemma
34
holds even if the sequence
\{x_{t,h}\}_{t\leq T}
was adversarially generated.

Lemma 34
.

Let
{f^{\star,m}}
satisfy Lemma
33
, and let
\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})
be defined in Algorithm
7
. Suppose Algorithm
7
is run on the data sequence
\{x_{t,h}\}_{t\leq 1}
, and let
Z_{t,h}
be defined in line
11
. Then, for any
\zeta>0
, with probability at least
1-M\delta
, for any
m\in[M]
, and
h\leq H
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t})\geq\zeta\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}}{\zeta^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},\nicefrac{{\zeta}}{{2}};{f^{\star,m}_{h}}),

where
\mathfrak{E}
denotes the eluder dimension given in Definition
1
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of Lemma
11
where we handle each
m\in[M]
and
h\in[H]
separately, and substitute the corresponding bounds for
{f^{\star,m}_{h}}
via Lemma
33
(instead of using Lemma
10
). We skip the proof for conciseness.
∎

E.2.2
Regret Bound

Suppose the trajectories at round
t
are generated using the deterministic dynamics
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,1},\dots,\mathbb{T}_{t,H}\}=\mathscr{T}(\cdot\penalty\ ;\penalty\ \iota_{t})
where
\iota_{t}
denotes the random seed that captures all of the stochasticity at round
t

11
11

11

We use random seed
\iota_{t}
to capture all the stochasticity in the choice of
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H,t\leq T}
. However, all our proofs extend to IL learning with an arbitrary, and possibly adversarial, choice of
\{\mathbb{T}_{t,h}\}_{h\leq H,t\leq T}
.

Recall that the policies
{\pi^{\star}}
and
{\pi_{t}}
such that for any
h\leq H
and
x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}
,

{\pi^{\star}}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{h}^{\star}(x)),
and,
{\pi_{t}}(x)=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t,h}(x)).

Note that Algorithm
7
collects trajectories using the policy
{\pi_{t}}
at round
t
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}=x_{t,h},

(103)

where
x_{t,h}
denotes the state at time step
h
in round
t
of Algorithm
7
. Finally, let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter. We start with the bound on the regret at time
t
.

Step 1: Bounding the difference in cumulative return at round
t
.

Fix any
t\leq T
, and let
\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}}
and
\tau_{t}^{\pi^{\star}}
denote the trajectories that would have been sampled using the policies
{\pi_{t}}
and the policy
{\pi^{\star}}
at round
t
. Furthermore, define the set
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
as

\displaystyle\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon={}\bigcup_{h=1}^{H}\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}\mid{}\mathtt{Query}(F_{h}^{\star}(x),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=1\}

(104)

Using Lemma
27
for the policies
{\pi_{t}}
and
{\pi^{\star}}
, and the set
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
defined above, we get that

\displaystyle R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi^{\star}}})-R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}})

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}),x_{t,h}^{{\pi_{t}}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.08621pt+2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\mathtt{T}_{A}+2H\mathtt{T}_{B},

(105)

where the second line is obtained by using the relation
(
103
)
in the second line. The last line simply defines
\mathtt{T}_{A}
and
\mathtt{T}_{B}
to be the second and the third term in the previous line, respectively, without the
2H
multiplicative factor. We bound these two terms separately below:

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{A}
.
Using the definition of
\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}
from
(
104
)
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),x_{t,h}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h}),\mathtt{Query}(F_{h}^{\star}(x_{t,h}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{\exists m\in[m]\penalty\ :\penalty\ \|f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|>\varepsilon\},

where the last line follows from the fact that the definition of
\mathtt{Query}
and the fact that
{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})
implies that there exists some
m\in[M]
for which
\|f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|>\varepsilon
. The above implies that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{\|f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|>\varepsilon\}.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{B}
.
First note that Lemma
33
implies that with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
m\leq M
and
h\leq H
,

\displaystyle\|{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|\leq\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}).

(106)

Next, note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\}

(107)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}(F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))=0,{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\},

where in the last line follows from plugging in the query condition under which
Z_{t,h}=0
. However note that for any
h\leq H
for which
\mathtt{Query}(F_{t,h}(x_{t,h}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}))=0
, by the definition of
\mathtt{Query}
and the fact that
{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})
, there must exist some
m\in[M]
such that

\displaystyle\|{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})-f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})\|>\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h}).

However, the above contradicts
(
106
)
, and thus with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{B}=0.

(108)

Plugging the above bounds on
\mathtt{T}_{A}
and
\mathtt{T}_{B}
in
(
105
)
, we get that

\displaystyle R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi^{\star}}})-R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}})

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{\|f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|>\varepsilon\}.

(109)

Step 2: Aggregating over all time steps.

Using the bound in
(
109
)
for each round
t
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi^{\star}}})-R(\tau_{t}^{{\pi_{t}}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h}^{{\pi^{\star}}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+2H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{\|f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|>\varepsilon\}.

Using the fact that
\mathbf{1}\{a\geq b\}\leq\nicefrac{{a^{2}}}{{b^{2}}}
for any
a,b\geq 0
, and the definition of
T_{\varepsilon,h}
in the above, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+2H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\frac{\|f^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})-{f^{\star,m}_{h}}(x_{t,h})\|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{2H}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}.

(110)

where the last line follows from using the bound in Lemma
33
.

Plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}
and ignoring
\log
factors and constants, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{T}\lesssim H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{H}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h};T)}+\frac{MH^{2}}{\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\log(1/\delta).

Notice that
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above so the final bound follows by taking
\inf
over all feasible
\varepsilon
.

E.2.3
Total Number of Queries

Fix any
t\leq T
, and let
h_{t}
denote the first time step at round
t
for which
Z_{t,h_{t}}=1
, if such a time-step exists (and is set to be
H+1
otherwise). We first observe that for all
h\leq h_{t}
, we have
{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})={\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})
. To see this, note that

\displaystyle Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{\exists h<h_{t}:{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{h_{t}-1}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{h_{t}-1}Z_{t,h_{t}}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{h_{t}-1}\bar{Z}_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq\pi_{t}(x_{t,h})\}

where in second inequality above, we used the fact that
Z_{t,h}=0
(and thus
\bar{Z}_{t,h}=1
) for all
h<h_{t}
, by the definition of
h_{t}
. Observe that the right hand side in the last inequality above is equivalent to the term
(
107
)
in the bound on
\mathtt{T}_{B}
above (where sum is now till
h_{t}
instead of
H
). Thus, using the bound in
(
108
)
, we get that

\displaystyle Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{\exists h<h_{t}:{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})\neq{\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\}=0,

and thus

\displaystyle{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})={\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})\qquad\text{for all $h\leq h_{t}$}.

(111)

Next, let
\varepsilon>0
be a free parameter, and note that plugging in the definition of
h_{t}
, we get that the total number of samples is bounded as:

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}+H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon},\|\Delta^{m}_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt+H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\notin\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon},\|\Delta^{m}_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})\|_{\infty}>\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{T}_{C}+\mathtt{T}_{D}+\mathtt{T}_{E},

where
\mathtt{T}_{C}
,
\mathtt{T}_{D}
and
\mathtt{T}_{E}
are the first, second and the third term respectively in the previous line. We bound them separately below.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{C}
. Fix any
t\leq T
. Using the relation in
(
111
)
, note that
{\pi^{\star}}(x_{t,h})={\pi_{t}}(x_{t,h})
for all
h<h_{t}
. Thus, the corresponding trajectories would be identical till time step
h_{t}
, which implies that
x_{t,h_{t}}=x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h_{t}}
. Using this property in the
\mathtt{T}_{C}
, we get that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{C})_{t}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h_{t}}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{x^{{\pi^{\star}}}_{t,h}\in\mathtt{X}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h},

where the last line plugs in the definition of
T_{\varepsilon,h}
.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{D}
.
First note that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{D})_{t}

\displaystyle=H\mathbf{1}\{\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})\right)=1,\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t,h_{t}}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0,\sup_{m\in[M]}\Delta^{m}_{t}(x_{t,h_{t}})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}.

In the following, we will show that all the conditions in the above indicator can not hold simultaneously. First note that since
\mathtt{Query}\left(F_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}}),{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})\right)=1
, there exists an
\widetilde{F}
such that

\displaystyle\mathtt{SelectAction}(\widetilde{F}(x_{t,h_{t}})))\neq\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}}))

(112)

and

\displaystyle\forall m\in[M]:\qquad\|\widetilde{F}(x_{t,h_{t}})[:,m]-F_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})[:,m]\|\leq\Delta_{t,h_{t}}^{m}(x_{t,h_{t}}).

(113)

On the other hand, recall that Lemma
33
implies that

\displaystyle\forall m\in[M]:\qquad\|F^{\star}(x_{t,h_{t}})[:,m]-F_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})[:,m]\|\leq\Delta_{t,h_{t}}^{m}(x_{t,h_{t}}).

(114)

Since,
\sup_{m}\Delta_{t,h_{t}}^{m}(x_{t,h_{t}})\leq\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}
, an application of Triangle inequality along with the bounds
(
113
)
and
(
114
)
imply that

\displaystyle\forall m\in[M]:\qquad\|F^{\star}(x_{t,h_{t}})[:,m]-\widetilde{F}(x_{t,h_{t}})[:,m]\|\leq 2\Delta_{t,h_{t}}^{m}(x_{t,h_{t}})<\varepsilon.

(115)

But the above contradicts the fact that
\mathtt{Query}(F^{\star}(x_{t,h_{t}}),\varepsilon\vec{\mathbbm{1}})=0
since both
\widetilde{F}
and
F_{t}
satisfy the norm constraints in the definition of
\mathtt{Query}
, but we can not simultaneously have that

\displaystyle\mathtt{SelectAction}(F^{\star}(x_{t,h_{t}})))=\mathtt{SelectAction}(F_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}}))=\mathtt{SelectAction}(\widetilde{F}(x_{t,h_{t}})),

due to
(
112
)
. Thus, we must have that

\displaystyle(\mathtt{T}_{D})_{t}

\displaystyle=0.

\bullet

Bound on
\mathtt{T}_{E}
.
We note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{\|{\vec{\Delta}}_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})\|_{\infty}>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle=H\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{\exists m\in[M]:\Delta^{m}_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h_{t}}\mathbf{1}\{\Delta^{m}_{t,h_{t}}(x_{t,h_{t}})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t,h}\mathbf{1}\{\Delta^{m}_{t,h}(x_{t,h})>\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{4}}\},

where the last line simply upper bound the term for
h_{t}
by the corresponding terms for all
h\leq H
.

Using Lemma
34
to bound the term in the right hand side for each
m\in[M]
and
h\leq H
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{E}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{320H{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},\frac{\varepsilon}{8};{f^{\star,m}_{h}}).

Gathering the bound above, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle\leq H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{320H}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},\frac{\varepsilon}{8};{f^{\star,m}_{h}}).

Plugging in the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Psi^{\ell_{\phi}}_{\delta}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},T)}
and ignoring
\log
factors and constants, we get that

\displaystyle N_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim H\sum_{h=1}^{H}T_{\varepsilon,h}+\frac{H}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Reg}^{\mathrm{\ell_{\phi}}}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h};T)}\cdot\mathfrak{E}(\mathcal{F}^{m}_{h},\nicefrac{{\varepsilon}}{{8}};{f^{\star,m}_{h}})+\frac{MH^{2}}{\lambda^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\log(1/\delta).

Notice that
\varepsilon
is a free parameter above so the final bound follows by taking
\inf
over all feasible
\varepsilon
.
